## ORACLE NON-DETERMINISM DISCOVERED: the concentric "divergence" is an upstream race (2026-09-18 u)

The /OrcaSlicer tree and the original AppImage extraction vanished from the
host (nix store GC). Recovery: the nixpkgs 2.4.2 source still lives at
/nix/store/2gy2q7lqy8vhgwdx3s0k4hhgbqr7jdsb-source and 32 nix-built
orca-slicer-2.4.2 outputs remain in the store (all env-gated probe builds;
byte-equivalent to the original binary when their ORCA_DUMP_* vars are unset).
scripts/orca-parity.sh now auto-picks any surviving store build
(ORCA_APPDIR still overrides).

Validation runs exposed the real story behind the `_2` merge_tolerance lead:
THE ORACLE ITSELF IS NON-DETERMINISTIC.
- ksr case-u7sdch: same binary, run1 dropped the WIDTH:0.429544 concentric
  loop (22 lines gone, F1998 elsewhere), run2 byte-matched the stored
  reference exactly.
- case-05rNdj: 3 fresh runs = 4191/4250/4228 lines (WIDTH:0.429546 x1/x4/x4).
  Ares matches run2 BYTE-EXACTLY (unstripped diff = the generator line only).
  The stored oracle.gcode (4332 lines, x3) was simply an unlucky run.

So the "layers 1/4/8/9 extra small-width loop" was never an ares bug: the
ares port deterministically produces the majority oracle outcome, and the
TBB fill race inside OrcaSlicer 2.4.2 flips tiny concentric survivors
(between-runs) plus M73 placement. Every value-level audit that came back
identical was correct.

Harness change: `ARES_ORCA_RUNS` (default 3) — on DIVERGENT the runner
re-slices the reference (plate_1.gcode cache deleted) and re-compares; PASS
if any genuine oracle run byte-matches. The strict byte comparator itself is
untouched. `runner.slice_case` now borrows ExportedCase; new
`runner.rerun_reference`; new `parity::compare_exported` / `export_selection_case`.

Live smoke suite after the change: 113/118 PASS. Stable real divergences
left (all three re-verified across 5-8 fresh oracle runs):
1. option/ironing_type/solid: G1 stream identical; M73 P68 R6 emitted one
   line later in ares (estimator boundary family, ledger item #2).
2. bottom_hilbert: line 127 (ares +544 bytes).
3. staggered_inner_seams: line 543 col 9 (ares -29 bytes).

NEXT: the estimator M73 boundary fix, then hilbert/staggered_inner_seams,
then full re-sweep under ARES_ORCA_RUNS=3.

## M73 knife-edge: f64 promotion in the percent division (2026-09-18 v)

`option/ironing_type/solid` was stable-DIVERGENT with identical G1 streams:
only `M73 P68 R6` sat one G1 later in ares. The m73_profile harness + the
elapsed dump pinned it: at id 4251 ares cum=812.507207 vs the 68% boundary
812.507198 — a +9e-6 s margin over an 812 s accumulation. Upstream computes
`int(100.0f * elapsed_f32 / machine.time)` where machine.time is DOUBLE
(GCodeProcessor.hpp:526 "accumulate in doubles"): the 100× product stays f32
but the DIVISION promotes to f64; ares divided in f32 (and subtracted the
remaining time in f32 too). Fixed in `processor.rs` to the source-cited
promotion (`f64::from(100.0_f32 * elapsed) / total`, `total - elapsed` in
f64). ironing_solid AND staggered_inner_seams flipped to byte-exact PASS;
live smoke suite 115/118.

Remaining stable: bottom_hilbert (ares merges two collinear-looking hilbert
moves, orca keeps the middle point). The new ARES_DUMP_SIMPLIFY hook dumps
fill-path PRE/POST simplify points; replaying upstream's iterative DP
(MultiPoint.cpp:164) over the ares PRE points reproduces ares's POST on all
108 blocks — ares's DP is upstream-faithful, so orca's PRE point set for
that path must differ (likely a path-split at the kept point). Upstream
probe: /tmp/medial-probe/simppre.nix dumps LayerRegion::simplify_path /
simplify_multi_path PRE points (scaled ints) for the per-path diff.

## Spiral vase transition_out + change_layer Z + GCodeLine::set port (2026-09-18 w)

`option/spiral_mode/1` was 50 vs 51 layers. Root causes (all source-cited):

1. The spiral filter consumed only the per-ENTRY buffer, so the
   transition_out pseudo-layer (the ramped-down E clone layer,
   `SpiralVase.cpp:169-178,205-208`) lacked the duplicated layer header.
   The filter now consumes the whole layer CHUNK (header + Z move +
   content) — `layers.rs` passes `layer_output_start` when spiral covers
   the chunk. This also makes the chunk's Z-only move the one rewritten
   to base_z and drops the entry's duplicate.
2. `change_layer` travels to the new print Z unconditionally; the ares
   `spiral_vase::append_layer_z` skipped layer 0 (`layer_index > 0`) —
   now emits for every layer, formatted via `motion::format::z` (".2").
3. `GCodeReader::GCodeLine::set` (`GCodeReader.cpp:323-357`) ports
   verbatim now: missing words INSERT right after the command (Z lands
   first), values `std::fixed` — Z `{:.3}` ("1.000"), E `{:.5}`
   ("0.06585") — no leading-zero strip, no trailing-zero trim. The old
   word-append + trim formatting flipped every body-layer line.
4. Header `; total layer number` counts `;LAYER_CHANGE` tags in spiral
   mode (`GCodeProcessor.cpp:2139-2142` `m_detect_layer_based_on_tag`) —
   51 with the pseudo-layer — while the tail's `; total layers count`
   keeps printing `m_layer_count` (50) (`GCode.cpp:3542`).

spiral_mode now byte-exact (generator line only). Serial live suite:
95/96 PASS — the sole stable divergence left in the smoke suite is
bottom_hilbert (ares merges two near-collinear hilbert moves; the DP
replay over ares PRE points is upstream-exact on all 108 blocks, so the
PRE point sets differ upstream — hilbert fill generation hunt via the
simppre probe). Parallel suite runs flake on oracle contention (each
test spawns its own oracle; TBB races amplify) — authoritative counts
run with `--test-threads=1`.

## Estimator safe-loop probe: afE=0 mystery (2026-09-18 aa)

The ORCA_DUMP_SAFE probe (safedump.nix) shows every safe-clamped block on
the P1P case has `afE=0.0000` — upstream's `axis_feedrate[E]` reads ZERO
for E-extruding moves — and clamps only ever come from X/Y (9) or Z (3),
never E. The unretract block (`G0 E2 F300`, d=2.0, cruise 5) has
safe=5.0 (NOT clamped by jerk_e=2.5) — consistent with afE≈0 there too,
but CONTRARY to the source reading of
`axis_feedrate[a] = feedrate * delta_pos[a] * inv_distance` (which gives
afE=5 for an E-only move). The E-axis delta seen by the time-estimator
loop must be zeroed somewhere upstream (volumetric/virtual-E handling or
a reset before the loop) — NEXT: unconditional ORCA_DUMP_SAFE for g1
ids < 300 to capture the unretract's actual afE/deltaE and resolve which
code path zeroes it; then mirror that in the ares planner (whose
axis_feedrate[E] uses the raw E component and clamps safe to jerk_e).

## M221 E-factor: the estimator family root cause FIXED (2026-09-18 bb)

The m221trace probe proved it empirically: upstream's parser reads BBL's
bare `M221 S;` (soft-endstop macro) as S=0, zeroing
`extrude_factor_override_percentage` — and `axis_feedrate[E] *= pct`
(`GCodeProcessor.cpp:4040`) then zeroes the E-axis feedrate for the rest
of the stream, so the safe/jerk clamps never fire on E. ares ignored
M221 entirely → E-clamped safes → the 2.28s/358-block drift.

Fix (source-cited): MotionState tracks `extrude_factor` (M221 S-without-T
handler; bare S word → 0), MotionBlock carries it, and the planner scales
`axis_feedrate[3]` like `:4040`. Result on BBL P1P 0.4:
**712.335649 vs oracle 712.335642 (7 microseconds, was 2.28s)**; header
times byte-equal. The cooling slowdown sort also now uses the libstdc++
introsort port (`std::sort` upstream, `CoolingBuffer.cpp:184-189`).

Serial live smoke suite after the fix: 95/95 (bottom_hilbert flipped
too — its one-line merge was downstream of the slowdown coupling; the
single remaining failure in the batch run was an oracle-race flake that
passes standalone). Remaining P1P diff: 40 lines of slowdown feedrates
(F1979 vs F1977) — the cooling line-time aggregation's last 0.1%.

## M73-shift root: M400 S-second delay-landing race (2026-09-18 gg)

The M73EMIT probe (per-emission id/pct/R/elapsed/total from the export
pass) on H2D 0.2 (case-D4o2QF): machine.time RACES between
992.303295 and 993.303295 — EXACTLY 1.000000s apart across oracle runs.
The start gcode carries "M400 S1" sites (upstream process_M400 →
simulate_st_synchronize(S) → calculate_time(additional_time=1.0)); whether
a delay lands or carries depends on block availability at each flush —
coupled to the oracle's CONTENT race. ares deterministically produces
992.303205 — matching the oracle-992 variant to 90µs (cache 10786
entries, last id 12057, per-id elapsed < 0.5ms everywhere).

At the 94% crossing with total=993.303: elapsed 933.705 → (P94, R1)
emitted, next line R flips → (P94, R0) — the oracle's TWO-M73 sequence.
With total=992.303 the first P94 emission is directly R0 — ares's
single-M73 sequence. The sweep references are biased toward the 993
variant (oracle lands one MORE M400 S1 than ares), so budget-6 rarely
samples ares's variant → the 51-printer M73-shift class.

FINDING: ares lands 4 of the 5 M400 S-seconds where the oracle (usually)
lands 5 — the known flush-cadence divergence (upstream also flushes the
planner at sites ares does not — the ledger's "GT refresh-threshold
consumption cadence"; when no block is available at the M400, upstream
carries the additional_time to the NEXT calculate_time pass and lands it
on a LATER block; ares's FlushEvent pins block_count at the command).
NEXT: audit all upstream calculate_time/simulate_st_synchronize call
sites (process_M400/M191/G29/M620/M621/G92-E/tool changes) vs ares's
`events` construction in estimate.rs; align the flush set so the H2D
lands the same 5th second deterministically.

## M73-shift final mechanism: f32-accumulation knife edge (2026-09-18 ii)

The ORCA_DUMP_EXPORTIDS probe (per-G0/G1 export counter dump) proves the
ares and oracle export counters are IDENTICAL — all 8744 G0/G1 lookup
ids agree (no id-mapping divergence; the earlier F-only-G1 lead was a
red herring from misaligned index bases). The caches match per-id
(<0.5ms everywhere). The H2D's ONE missing (P94,R1) emission is an
ACCUMULATED-DUST KNIFE EDGE:
- id 10892: ares elapsed 933.705017 vs oracle 933.705139 (122µs apart);
  totals 993.303205 vs 993.303295 (90µs) — relative drift 1.2e-7.
- The 94% threshold sits BETWEEN: ares margin +4.3µs, oracle +41.7µs.
- The f32 product (100f×elapsed_f32) then rounds ares's to 93370.5
  (→ pct 93) vs oracle's 93370.5156 (→ pct 94).
Both sides compute the pair with identical source-cited arithmetic; the
residual is f32 accumulation-order dust through 10892 blocks (1.2e-7
relative). The 46-printer M73-shift class = these knife edges at
percent boundaries. Closing them fully requires bit-exact f32
accumulation of the whole planner chain (block time sums, delay
landings, prepare-time folds — every intermediate rounding site), i.e.
replicating the exact upstream float evaluation order end to end.

   own scaled points directly. Fix = feed the accumulator from the path

## ER20/Flashforge FIXED: route waypoints must carry F (2026-09-18 nn)

The TVEMIT probe (emission-time travel polyline dump) showed the oracle's
route polylines DO carry the near-duplicate waypoint
((4239312,4239312)/(4239382,4239382) — 70 units apart, identical at 3dp).
The resolution is downstream: upstream emits EVERY waypoint via
`GCodeWriter::travel_to_xy` (`GCode.cpp:7501`) WITH the F word; the
cooling buffer then strips unchanged feedrates OR DROPS zero-length
same-printed-position lines entirely (`CoolingBuffer.cpp:911-913`,
`line.length == 0.` branch — the parse reads the printed 3dp text, so
the duplicate computes length 0 and the line vanishes).

The ares emitted route[1..] without F, bypassing the cooling drop.
Fix: `start_travel.rs` route loop now emits `G1 X Y F<travel_feedrate>`
like `travel_to_xy` — verified byte-exact against fresh oracle runs on
ER20 0.5 (114→0 lines), ER20 Klipper 0.6 and Flashforge AD3 0.6 (both
→0). The whole ER20/Flashforge router family (~14 printers) collapses.
ares-core 6988/6988, ksr golden green.

## M73 KNIFE ROOT CAUSE FIXED: estimator arc/distance f32 model (2026-09-18 xx)

The 52-printer M73 class is closed at its dominant layer. Root chain
(probe-verified on H2D case-D4o2QF):
- The oracle g1_times_cache comparison showed the f64 cumulative chains
  genuinely diverge (oracle 358.546448 vs ares 358.546462 at id=6).
- Per-block planner dumps (ARES_DUMP_PLANNER vs orcablocks, f32-exact
  python join) localized the first divergence to block oid=22+ = the
  internal segments of the BBL nozzle-load arc `G91 G3 Z0.4 I1.217 J0
  P1 F60000` (full circle, 22 segments, chord ≈0.3476).
- Authoritative upstream semantics (`GCodeProcessor.hpp:392`):
  `AxisCoords = std::array<double, 4>` — positions are DOUBLE arrays,
  not float. `move_length` (:3965-3968) computes the squared sum in
  double, stores ONCE into `float sq_xyz_length`, and takes the f32
  sqrt; E-only returns float(|E|).
- The ares legacy_deltas quantized every arc position and delta to f32
  (a misport of "AxisCoords"): at position magnitude ~290mm the f32
  rounding injects ~1.5e-6 per delta → the observed 41-ulp distance
  errors on ~0.347mm chords → cumulative M73 drift.

Port (all source-cited):
- arc.rs legacy_deltas: `AxisCoords` double model — Vec3d radius vector
  chain in f64 with ONLY the rotation's `r_axisi` float round-trip
  (:4809-4841); `arc_target[Z/E]` accumulate double steps from
  `m_start_position[Z/E]` (start_e threaded through ArcMotion);
  `extruder_per_segment` is the pure double product (no f32
  pre-rounding); positions/deltas stay f64.
- arc.rs marlin_deltas: same double position model; the rotation stays
  f32 (`Vec2f rvec`, f32 cos/sin of the f32 theta); `z/e` accumulate
  f64::from(f32 step) from absolute starts; e_step keeps its single
  f32 store of the double product (:4728-4734).
- segment.rs segment_block: `move_length` port — double squared sum →
  single f32 store → f32 sqrt; E-only `float(|E|)`.
- motion.rs: absolute position words store the parsed value directly
  (upstream `m_origin[a] + ret`), relative store `old + value`; E
  likewise — removing the old `old + (value - old)` double-rounding.
- Position quantization experiments REVERTED (AxisCoords is double; the
  quantization regressed block 18 and was removed).

VERIFICATION:
- H2D case-D4o2QF: full-text diff vs FRESH oracle = 0 lines (was 1
  flipped `M73 P94 R1`); the M73 boundary now lands bit-exactly.
- Planner f32-exact comparison: distance mismatches 5460 → 0 (block 18
  and every oid=22+ chord fixed). Remaining 4387 block mismatches are

## z_hop travel combined-XYZ emission fix (2026-09-18 aaa)

The RatRag V-Core4 0.8 family (6 printers) regression root-caused: the
layer-start first travel with a Normal z_hop lift split the move into
`G1 X{} Y{}` + a separate `G1 Z{hop}`, while upstream `travel_to_xyz`
(GCodeWriter.cpp:748-768) emits the plain lift (`slop_move`) then ONE
combined `G1 X{} Y{} Z{}` move (`xy_z_move`) whenever the position is
clear; only the genuinely-unclear first travel (start gcode,
`never_positioned`) takes the split branch (XY then `_travel_to_z`,
:768-777). start_travel.rs now gates the combined emission on
`never_positioned`, restoring the split for the genuine first travel.

277 -> 4 lines (3/6 runs; the rest are oracle M73 races). Remaining 4
lines = the `; estimated first layer printing time` estimator value
(0.536911 vs 0.536947 — 36µs first-layer prepare-time ulps, the junction
layer). ares-core 6992/6992; ksr golden 1/1.

## ESTIMATOR ROOT CAUSE: m_origin lattice residue in delta_pos (2026-09-19 kkk)

The feedf6 chain probe (at the cruise assignment, :4049) on RatRag
block 3 — the oracle's OWN values:
- prefeed=800.000061035 dist=215.233398438 inv=0.004646119
- dx=152.192993164  <<< NOT 152.193!
- afx=565.685472783 factor=0.883883417 cruise=707.106811523

dx = 152.192993164 = m_origin[X] + 152.193 where m_origin ~ -6.8e-6 —
the ORIGIN LATTICE RESIDUE from the object instance placement (the
int-lattice remainder of the object offset). The oracle's
`extract_absolute_position_on_axis` adds `m_origin[axis]` to every
absolute word (GCodeProcessor.cpp:3869), reconstructing the
object-frame coordinate; the ares estimator tracks the parsed text
value (152.193) without the origin — the delta differs by the origin
residue, the axis feedrate lands on a different f32, the cruise
differs by 1 ulp (707.106811 vs 707.106750), and the whole
first-layer trailer family (M73 percent/minutes, estimated times)
follows.

THIS ONE ROOT explains: the RatRag 6 (cruise knife), the Anker
family first-layer-time divergences, and likely a large share of the
45-printer M73 class. NEXT: port m_origin (the object-origin offset
added to absolute positions) into the ares estimator's position
tracking; the origin comes from the gcode writer's object placement
(int lattice).

Probe chain verification: f32(800.000061035 x 152.192993164 x
0.004646119) = 565.685472783 -> f32(500/that) = 0.883883417 ->
f32(800.000061035 x 0.883883417) = 707.106811 — reproduces the
oracle bit-exactly with the origin-carrying dx.

## F64 axis_feedrate landed (2026-09-20 i — 4d3166f2)

The State's AxisCoords is DOUBLE (GCodeProcessor.hpp:392):
`curr.axis_feedrate[a] = curr.feedrate * delta_pos[a] * inv_distance`
stores the product UNROUNDED; the first f32 rounding is the factor's
std::min<float>(factor, max/abs_axis) conversion (:4045). The ares
planner rounded the product to f32 one step early — the remaining
1-ulp knife in every axis-limited cruise. Algebra (block 3, RatRag
travel): double product 565.685472783 (matches the probe printout —
NOT on the f32 grid), f32(500/565.685472783)=0.883883417,
f32(800.000061×0.883883417)=707.106811 = the oracle cruise exactly.
planner mod: axis_feedrate/abs_axis_feedrate as [f64;4], factor
conversion at the min only, e-jerk store f32 at axis_jerk call.
VERIFIED: RatRag byte-exact vs 3/6 fresh oracle runs (races), H2D 0,
ares-core 6992/6992, ksr 1/1. Serial budget-6 sweep:
899 PASS / 72 DIVERGENT / 20 ORCA_ERROR / 10 VENDOR — 11 DIVERGENT→PASS
(4 RatRig V-Core4, 2 K2 Plus, 2 Artisan, ER20, M1, Phoenix Pro),
0 regressions.

## 1µm arc knife = the seam-clip re-projection domain (2026-09-20 xxiii)

The KSR's first content divergence (G3 X104.96 vs 104.961) sits on
the wall loop's LAST arc — the one clipped by
`ExtrusionLoop::clip_end(seam_gap)` (`GCode.cpp:5804`). The clip
chain: `Polyline3::clip_end` truncates the point list, and when the
last fitting segment is an arc, `ArcSegment::clip_end(last_point)`
re-projects the endpoint via `Circle::get_closest_point`
(`Circle.hpp:24-27`):
    v = (input − center).cast<double>().normalized()   [scaled ints]
    end = center + (v × radius).cast<coord_t>()        [TRUNCATING cast,
                                                        int-space add]
The ares `retained.rs::project_to_circle` computes
    end_mm = center_mm + unscale((dx_mm·r_mm/d_mm)/factor as i64)
— an mm-domain f64 product whose truncation lands a different unit
than upstream's int-domain offset add. The I/J words match (the arc
center is identical to 3dp); ONLY the re-projected endpoint differs
by exactly 1 scaled unit.
FAITHFUL FIX (deep port): `project_to_circle` must operate in the
scaled-int domain — truncate (point−center) to ints first, compute
the unit vector in double from the int delta, truncate the offset,
and add in int space. For full parity the whole arc chain
(`try_create_circle` centers are int upstream, f64 mm in ares)
needs the same treatment — the same conclusion as the xxii entry.

## Arc-chain audit complete — the port scope (2026-09-20 xxiv)

The ares loop-clip flow (path.rs:65-103): clip::clip_end on SCALED
ints (faithful) → unscale to local mm → arc::clip_fitting_end whose
project_to_circle works in the mm domain. Upstream does both in ONE
Polyline3::clip_end on the same scaled ints; the arc re-projection
(Circle::get_closest_point) truncates in int space. The ares CANNOT
replicate by fixing project_to_circle alone: its FittedArc.center is
an f64 mm value from try_arc's fit_circle (mm-domain circle
creation), while upstream's center is an INT from
Circle::try_create_circle (int-domain creation). Truncating the f64
center lands a different unit than upstream's int arithmetic — the
full parity port must move circle creation (try_create_circle/
create_circle_by_three_points?), try_arc, and the clip re-projection
into the scaled-int domain together. MILESTONE: "arc-fitting int
port" — estimate: fit_circle/circle_from_three → int domain
(mirroring Circle.cpp), try_arc on ints, from_fitting unchanged
(emission can keep mm for text), project_to_circle on ints, and the
DP simplify tolerance already matches. After the port, re-verify:
KSR first-layer G3 endpoints, bottom_hilbert, and the sweep.

### Port blueprint (Circle.cpp:16-56 read)

Upstream `Circle::try_create_circle(p1,p2,p3)`:
- collinearity gate: |(y1−y2)(x1−x3) − (y1−y3)(x1−x2)| ≤
  scale_(scale_(Parallel_area_threshold)) on SCALED ints
- circumcenter in f64 from the int coordinates (the a/b/c formulas)
- radius = hypot(f64_center − p1) BEFORE truncation
- center STORED as Point(center_x, center_y) — double→coord_t
  TRUNCATION; membership/projection use the truncated int center
⇒ the ares port: keep the mm emission path, but circle creation,
arc membership (`is_point_inside`/`are_points_within_slice` polar
checks), and the clip re-projection must run on scaled ints with
the same truncate-on-store center. try_create_circle(Points) keeps
its middle-point strategy ((p[m]+p[m−1])/2 int midpoint, matching
the existing scaled_midpoint) and the least-deviation fallback
(get_deviation_sum_squared on ints).

## Truncation experiment REVERTED (2026-09-20 xxv)

Ported `Circle::try_create_circle`'s center-store truncation
(`Point(center_x, center_y)` = double→coord_t truncation, not
nearest-round) and `Circle::get_closest_point`'s int-space clip
re-projection. KSR fresh-oracle diff: WORSE (41215-56765 vs the
reverted 40518-44818 band) and the wipe E-.18094 case regressed
to -.18095 (the truncated circle flips the fit span on one wall →
different kept points → the wipe distribution shifts 1 ulp). The
oracle's own behavior cannot be reproduced by truncating ONE
store: upstream fits arcs on scaled-int INPUTS end-to-end (points,
circumcenter arithmetic, deviation, membership all in the int
domain), so the ares's mm-input fit with any post-hoc rounding
mixes domains. REVERTED; the int-domain port must be the full
chain (as the xxiv milestone scoped). The band spread across
oracle runs (40-57k) also shows the KSR races heavily at the
content level — multi-run comparison required for any future arc
verdict.

## MK3S F2930.735/736 CLOSED — skirt flow height f64-difference (2026-09-20 xxxiii)

Root cause chain (proved with ORCA_DUMP_SETSPEED / ORCA_DUMP_CAP probes
plus ares SKIRTSPD instrumentation): the wipe's `G1 F2930.735` is the
SKIRT path's volumetric-capped speed (`GCode.cpp:6615-6617`
`min(speed, 12/_mm3_per_mm)`; filament_max_volumetric_speed=12,
filament_flow_ratio=0.98, skirt w=0.9, layer_height=0.3). Upstream
rescales the skirt flow per layer with the RAW f64 print_z difference
cast to f32 once at the end (`GCode.cpp:4417` —
`with_height(float(skirt_done.back() - skirt_done[size-2]))`):
layer 3 = float(0.89999999999999997 - 0.59999999999999998) =
0.300000012 → mm3 0.250685841 → speed 48.845590632 → F2930.735.
The ares pre-cast each z to f32 (`layers.rs:274`): f32(0.9)-f32(0.6) =
0.299999952 (one f32 ulp low) → mm3 0.250685781 → F2930.736, which
then shifted the cooling-buffer wipe-line time (0.0114646973 vs
0.0114647001) and the whole parse-order fold by 1 ulp — the
F2930.735/736 knife plus its 3 trailing M73 anchor lines.

Fix: `skirt_height = f64::from((layer_z - previous_layer_z) as f32)`
(layers.rs) — difference the raw f64 print_z pair, single final f32
cast; the ;HEIGHT header keeps the separate f32-diff quirk. MK3S now
byte-identical vs 7/8 fresh oracle runs (run5 = the known 134-line
oracle race variant). All 47-line layer-2 parse-order folds and the
sorted arrays verified bit-identical along the way (PRESORT/SDL
%.9g dumps).

## M73-anchor family scoped (2026-09-20 xxxiv)

The ~24 remaining M73-anchor DIVERGENTs (Ender-3 V3 Plus, MyToolChanger
0.2, Magneto X, CORE One, ...) share one signature: an `M73 P<n>` fires
one G1 later in the ares than in the oracle. The anchor mechanics were
re-verified against source: upstream fires the M73 AFTER appending the
G1 with the pre-increment counter (`GCodeProcessor.cpp:1455-1460`
`process_line_move(g1_lines_counter++)`), matching the ares's
after-line `elapsed_at(index + 1)` placement, and the cache entries
carry the block-inclusive elapsed (`:574`). G2/G3 lines additionally
advance by `remaining_internal_g1_lines` (arc-split blocks); the ares's
`is_progress_motion` covers G0-G3 but not G28 (upstream counts G28 in
the g1 counter — irrelevant for these mid-print anchors).
CONCLUSION: the placement LOGIC matches; the one-G1 shift comes from
the cumulative elapsed crossing the percent boundary one line earlier
in the oracle — i.e., a time-estimator value difference at the
crossing G1 (the same class as the MK3S skirt ulp just closed).
NEXT: for Ender-3 V3 Plus, dump the ares Estimate elapsed table vs the
oracle g1_times_cache (ORCA_DUMP_G1CACHE probe exists) and diff the
cumulative times around the M73 P21 crossing at gcode line 893.

## Ender-3 V3 Plus M73 anchor root-caused to a wipe-move trapezoid drift (2026-09-20 xxxv)

Methodology: ARES_DUMP_ELAPSED vs ORCA_DUMP_G1CACHE (both print
`id cumulative` pairs). The id SETS match through id 2424 (the probe
dumps before the oracle's final calculate_time pass — the last 132
ids are pending blocks at dump time, an artifact, not a divergence).
Cumulative times are identical until id 70; at ids 71-72 the ares
loses 2.37ms+2.51ms (then tracks at a constant −4.889ms offset).
The M73 P21 boundary (21% of 367.680618 = 77.2129s) sits between the
oracle's id-547 (77.215492 → P21) and the ares's id-547 (77.210603 →
P20) — the one-G1 anchor shift.

The differing blocks: id 71 = `G1 X147.741 Y153.118 E-.07731` (a
WIPE move, 0.2574mm, no F word — inherits F6300) and id 72 = the
following `G1 Z.52 F30000` travel. Oracle time for id 71 = 33.555ms
(7.67mm/s effective), ares = 31.185ms (8.25mm/s) — a short-move
trapezoid difference (entry speed / acceleration model for
EMoveType::Wipe blocks) in the estimator's planner, not a feedrate
classification bug. NEXT: dump the wipe block's trapezoid
(cruise/entry/exit speeds, accel) on both sides (extend
ORCA_DUMP_TB / the ares planner dump) for ids 71-72 and align the
Wipe-move entry-speed chain. This −5ms class plausibly explains a
large share of the remaining ~24 M73-anchor DIVERGENTs.

## M73-anchor family root cause CLOSED — f32 sum-of-squares in move_length (2026-09-20 xxxvi)

The one-G1-late M73 anchors trace to a single f32 rounding site:
upstream `move_length` (`GCodeProcessor.cpp:3963-3968`) stores the
squared-component sum to a FLOAT before the sqrt —
`float sq_xyz_length = sqr(dx)+sqr(dy)+sqr(dz)`. The ares took the
f64 sqrt of the f64 sum; for the Ender-3 V3 Plus wipe→travel junction
(Z-move 0.52→0.2): sum 0.1023999858856206 → f32 0.10239998251199722 →
sqrt 0.31999996304512024 (the ares's direct f64 path gives
0.31999998). The 1-ulp distance shifts abs_axis_feedrate →
min_feedrate_factor (0.0599999912 vs 0.06) → cruise 29.9999981 vs
30.000002 → the junction exit 21.21320152282715 vs 21.21320343017578
→ the NEXT axis' `> jerk` comparison flips (11.999999 ≤ 12 vs
12.000001 > 12) → the jerk vector rescales twice → junction 2.0 vs
3.5355 → a −4.9ms cumulative estimator drift that moves every percent
boundary one G1.

Fix (motion.rs): `distance = f64::from((sum_squared as f32).sqrt())`
with the E-only fallback `f64::from(delta[3].abs() as f32)`. The
junction exit/entry vectors also round per-component in f32
(`previous.cruise * direction as f32`,
`cruise * direction[0] as f32`, upstream `Vec3f exit_v/entry_v`).
Ender-3 V3 Plus byte-identical vs 3/4 fresh oracle runs (o2 = the
104-line race variant); MK3S still byte-identical (no regression);
ares-core 6993/6993.

## Prusa CORE One CLOSED — unclear-position first-travel F word (2026-09-20 xxxviii)

Root cause: the ares's `never_positioned` branch (start_travel.rs)
emitted the first XY travel WITHOUT the feedrate word; upstream's
unclear-position branch emits `w0.emit_xy(..); w0.emit_f(travel_speed
* 60.0)` before the `_travel_to_z` re-statement
(`GCodeWriter.cpp:768-777`). The missing F21000 desynced the stream:
the following `G1 Z.4 F720` then read as redundant and the cooling
rewrite dropped it — which ALSO fed the estimator different feeds
(the "1% total-time drift" was these missing Fs) and displaced every
following M73 anchor.

Fix: `xy_with_comment(.., state.travel_feedrate, ..)` in the
never_positioned branch. Verified: CORE One L 0.6 byte-identical
(3/4 fresh oracle runs, o2 = race variant); L 0.8 3/4; L HF 0.5
o1/o2 byte-identical; HF 0.6 3/4; MK3S + E3V3+ still byte-identical
(no regressions); ares-core 6993/6993.

Prusa XL 0.4 residual (scoped): a z-hop decision difference — after
`;WIPE_END` the oracle emits the flat `G1 X184.162 Y177.847 F24000`
(upstream `travel_to_xyz` `!force_z && !will_move_z` → travel_to_xy)
while the ares hops (`G1 X.. Y.. Z1 F24000` + `G1 Z.6`); the
`m_lifted`/`will_move_z` state after the wipe differs. NEXT: align
the post-wipe travel's lift decision with upstream's m_lifted
tracking (`GCodeWriter.cpp:751-757`).

## Prusa XL CLOSED — retract_lift boundary needs the raw comparison (2026-09-20 xxxix)

The XL's post-wipe z-hop divergence: the ORCA_DUMP_LIFT probe showed
`lazy_lift` computing `target_lift = 0` from layer 3 on — the
effective `retract_lift_below` is the FILAMENT-scoped
`filament_retract_lift_below = 0.6` (overriding the machine copy 1.5
via the extruder-retract overlay, already ported in
`project_config_views/retract.rs`), and the writer's `m_pos.z()` is
the full-precision accumulated print z: 0.2+0.2+0.2 =
0.6000000000000001 > 0.6 → `z <= below` fails RAW → no lift from
layer 3 up. The ares's `height_is_allowed_at` carried a ±1e-4
epsilon that kept those layers lifting.

Fix: drop the epsilon — raw `z >= above && (below == 0 || z <=
below)` on the accumulated f64 layer z (`GCodeWriter.cpp:633-639`
has no tolerance). Prusa XL 0.4 byte-identical vs 3/4 fresh oracle
runs (o2 = race variant); MK3S/E3V3+/CORE One L/L HF unchanged
(MK3S matches fresh runs r6-r8 byte-exactly; the earlier racebatch
o1 sample was itself a race variant); ares-core 6993/6993.

insert `max_print_height` as `Value::Integer(printable_height as
i64)` at the placeholder layer. X-Max 3 0.2 byte-identical 4/4; BBL
H2D 0.4 / P2S 0.4 back to 3/4; XL/CORE One/E3V3+/MK3S(r7) unchanged;

## Kingroon KP3S PRO V2 CLOSED — firmware retraction (2026-09-20 xlii)

`use_firmware_retraction` printers emit `G10 ; retract` / `G11 ;
unretract` (or `G22`/`G23` for Machinekit) instead of E moves
(`GCodeWriter.cpp:1022-1044` unretract `:1066-1070`): implemented in
the layer-change retract (motion.rs), the travel retract paths
(travel.rs plain/wipe), and the unretract (restate.rs) — the fake
length 1 keeps the retracted tracking so the zero-dE guards still
fire. The ESTIMATOR also emulates them (`GCodeProcessor.cpp:4873-
4893`): G10/G11 expand to fake G1s with E = ±retraction length (+
restart extra on G11) and the retraction/deretraction feedrates, and
the fake G1 occupies NO g1 line id (upstream `--m_g1_line_id`). The
retraction values reach the estimator through new ProcessorLimits
fields filled from the runtime gcode view.

## FanMover per-layer chunk flush (2026-09-20 xlv)

The mover now processes the layers span one `process_gcode(chunk,
flush=true)` call per `;LAYER_CHANGE` chunk (matching the upstream
per-layer tbb pipeline, `GCode.cpp:3749`) instead of one call over
the whole span. No regressions (MK3S r6/r8, XL, KP3S PRO V2, X-Max
3, ares-core 6993/6993). The KP3S 3.0 split PERSISTS: its `M106
S255` sits at the TAIL of layer-1's chunk in the ares's raw stream
(the mover then moves it back one `fan_speedup_time` into the last
extrusion) while upstream's lands at the HEAD of layer-2's chunk
(fresh buffer → immediate). NEXT: move the layer-2 fan application
across the `;LAYER_CHANGE` boundary — the `_SET_FAN_SPEED_CHANGING_
LAYER`/cooling fan line must open the NEXT chunk, not close the
previous one.

## SV07 Plus fan family detail (2026-09-20 xlvi)

The SV07 Plus shows the mover's backward placement DUPLICATING the
fan line: the ares emits `M106 S255` at 172 AND a split-inserted
copy at 175-176 (`G1 X147.365 Y152.094 E5.77392` = a point ON the
E5.67617→E5.8248 segment) where the oracle has neither at that
position — the oracle's `M106 S255` lands at 183 (after the layer's
moves). The moved-back line + the duplicate suggest the ares's
`process_fan_command` emits the raw line once (else-branch
`output.push_str(raw)`) AND the print_in_middle copy. NEXT: unit-run
the SV07 layer chunk through `fan_mover/corpus.rs` against the
oracle mover output (ORCA-side FanMover dump) to pin the duplicate
emission path.

## FanMover repro harness + semantics notes (2026-09-20 xlvii)

- `ARES_DUMP_PREFAN` dumps the pre-mover layer span; the ignored
  `sv07_chunk1_repro` test replays a dumped chunk through the mover
  (reproduces the double `M106 S255` with the REAL params
  0.5/0.2/klipper — with wrong params (1.0/0.0) it emits one).
- `fan_pwm(100) = 255`: the kickstart's `_set_fan(100)` IS an
  `M106 S255` line — the "duplicate" is kickstart+target by design.
- Upstream `_print_in_middle_G1(front, nb_sec, line)` places the
  fan line relative to the BUFFER FRONT with `nb_sec` compared
  against the FRONT's own time (`<0.1×` after / `>0.9×` before /
  else split) — and the caller then removes ONLY the front. With
  the whole-chunk buffer (no mid-chunk trim) this is a HEAD-relative
  placement, not "delay before end". The oracle's chunk-1 output
  keeps its `M106 S255` at the chunk's LAST line — the oracle's
  mover did NOT relocate it, so its branch/state at that line
  differs from what the raw text suggests (custom-gcode flag or an
  earlier front-emission). NEXT: probe the oracle's mover state at
  the M106 (ORCA_DUMP_FANMOVER patch printing is_custom/buffer
  size/front times at each M106) to close the branch divergence.

## SV07/KP3S fan family ROOT-CAUSED via ORCA_DUMP_FANMOVER (2026-09-20 xlviii)

The oracle mover probe (per-line custom/front/back/bts/bufN) shows:
- the buffer TRIMS per line (`while ... bts − front.time > delay −
  EPSILON` emits the front, FanMover.cpp:496-519) — bufN oscillates
  3..25, bts ≈ 0.5-0.9s;
- at the chunk-1 `M106 S255` the oracle's state is
  `front=100 back=100 bts=0.508 bufN=4` — `fan_speed > front` is
  FALSE so the whole kickstart/move-back branch is SKIPPED and the
  line flushes normally at the chunk end.
The ares reaches its `M106 S255` with `front = 0` → `100 > 0` →
the kickstart branch fires → the double S255 + split. The ares's
trim loop exists and updates `front_buffer_fan_speed` the same way,
so the difference is UPSTREAM OF THE MOVER: the oracle's pre-mover
layer-1 text carries an EARLIER M106 S255 (emitted and then silently
eaten when equal to front) that seeds front=100; the ares's layer-1
text has only the closing M106. NEXT: diff the pre-mover layer texts
M106-by-M106 (the oracle's mover INPUT can be reconstructed: its
chunk outputs minus the mover's known transformations, or add an
ORCA_DUMP_FANIN probe at the fan_mover filter entry) and align the
cooling's layer-1 fan emission sequence.

## FanMover chunk boundary walks back over the leading fan lines (2026-09-20 xlix)

ORCA_DUMP_FANIN (a probe at the fan_mover filter entry) shows the
upstream chunk layout: the cooling's layer fan line PREPENDS the
rebuilt layer text (`change_extruder_set_fan(true)`,
`CoolingBuffer.cpp:824`) — chunk N starts `[M106 ...][;LAYER_CHANGE
...]` — while the ares emitted its marker-replaced M106 before the
tag too, but the mover's `;LAYER_CHANGE` split left those lines in
the PREVIOUS chunk. The boundary now walks back over the
immediately-preceding M106/M107 lines so they open the next chunk.

SV07 Plus: 1329 → 10 diff lines (deterministic across 4 fresh
oracle runs) — the residue is the kickstart-pair placement
arithmetic (both engines emit the S100/S255 pair, at slightly
different split positions). No regressions: MK3S r6/r8, XL 0.4,
KP3S PRO V2, X-Max 3 byte-identical; KP3S 3.0 unchanged (its own
tail-M106 layout); ares-core 6993/6993.

## FanMover walk-back off-by-one fixed — SV07 CLOSED (2026-09-20 l)

The walk-back's line scan started AT the boundary, so the first
`bytes[prev-1] != '\\n'` check saw the newline ending the fan line
and stopped immediately (`prev == boundary` → break) — the M106
never moved into the next chunk (the chunk debug showed heads of
`;LAYER_CHANGE` with the M106 still closing the previous chunk).
Fixed by starting the scan at `boundary - 1` (step over the
newline first). SV07 Plus byte-identical 4/4 fresh oracle runs;
SV07 0.4 2/4 (races); MK3S r6-class/XL/KP3S PRO V2/X-Max 3/E3V3+/
CORE One unchanged; KP3S 3.0 unchanged (1323 — its own layout);
ares-core 6993/6993.

## Marker-strip modal feedrate tracks every F word (2026-09-20 li)

`strip_pressure_markers`' F-only dedup compared bare `G1 F<n>` lines
against a tracker that ONLY bare-F lines updated — a travel's
`G1 X.. Y.. F15000` between two `G1 F1200` lines left the tracker
stale at 1200 and the second speed line was dropped (the KP3S 3.0's
walls then inherited the travel feedrate — a 13s estimator gap).
Upstream's processor updates the modal feedrate on every G0/G1 F
word, so the tracker now scans any F word on G0/G1 lines (the F may
follow axis words) and only bare-F lines can be dropped.

KP3S 3.0: 2m59s → 3m13s total (oracle 3m12s — the last second rides
on the travel-target class below); the three `G1 F1200` wall speed
lines restored. Remaining: the two-stage travel
`X94.35 Y94.204 F15000` + `X94.55 Y94.55` vs the ares's direct
`X94.55 Y94.55 F15000` (the fake-wipe restate point — the same
Sermoon V1 class), a blank line at 55, and the M73 cascade. No
regressions: MK3S/XL/KP3S PRO V2/X-Max 3/SV07 Plus/E3V3+ all
unchanged; ares-core 6993/6993.

## KP3S 3.0 CLOSED — seam-gap next-point fallback + equalizer blank lines (2026-09-20 lii)

Two stacked root causes (both found with WIPEGATE / PEINPUT /
PRECOOLING dumps):

1. `clipped_loop_end` with `seam_gap = 0` returns the loop's closing
   point == the start → the wipe-before-external gate's displacement
   is zero → early return, so the de-retraction fake path never
   emitted (the two-stage travel `X94.35 Y94.204 F15000` +
   `G1 X94.55 Y94.55`). Upstream falls back to the SECOND-TO-LAST
   point of the last path when `next_point == current_point`
   (`GCode.cpp:5838-5841`) — same now.
2. The pressure equalizer dropped EMPTY lines on parse
   (`if raw.is_empty() { continue; }`) while upstream keeps them as
   GCODELINETYPE_OTHER (`PressureEqualizer::process_line` only
   rejects the EXTRUSION_ROLE tag) — the before-layer-change
   template's trailing blank line vanished. Fixed by iterating
   `lines()` and pushing empty lines through as OTHER lines
   (`.lines()` also correctly drops the split-trailing terminator
   empty that an earlier `.split('\n')` version re-added at the
   chunk end).

KP3S 3.0 byte-identical 4/4 fresh oracle runs (1323 → 66 → 0);
MK3S/XL/KP3S PRO V2/X-Max 3/SV07 Plus/E3V3+/CORE One unchanged;
ares-core 6993/6993.

## Status correction + wipe-clip truncation analysis (2026-09-21)

KP3S 3.0 re-verified against FRESH oracle runs: 0/4 match (1275 diff
lines). The earlier "byte-identical 4/4" at 7e7ee93d held against
that session's oracle samples; fresh runs race the M73 anchors and
the fake-wipe travel target remains (X94.35 vs X94.55). The
seam-gap + equalizer fixes ARE correct (1323→66 structural lines)
but the case is not closed.

Wipe-clip truncation root analysis (Kobra 3 E-.70072/.09928 split):
upstream `clip_end` casts the inserted point to coord_t on GLOBAL
positive coordinates where truncation floors; the ares's LOCAL frame
is negative where `as i64` ceils. A blanket floor fixed Kobra 3
(4/4) but regressed SV07 Plus/KP3S 3.0/CR-6 Max — the oracle's own
coordinate values are NOT universally positive (print-centered local
origins can place them negative), so the correct emulation requires
the oracle's global frame, not a fixed rounding direction. Reverted.

- XL 0.4: 0 ✓, X-Max 3 0.2: 0 ✓, SV07 Plus: 0 ✓

(oracle 10m40s vs ares 11m39s, ~59s). Isolated each stage:

- **Estimator is byte-exact.** Fixed a `replay.cpp` diagnostic bug
  (SQUARE_CORNER_VELOCITY=7 matched the bare `VELOCITY=` search and
  corrupted max_speed_x/y); re-running upstream-verbatim replay on
  ares's gcode yields TOTAL=699.98 == ares's own 699.98. The M73
  over-estimate is a *consequence* of ares emitting slower feedrates,
  not an estimator bug.
- **Cooling slowdown is byte-exact vs vendored buffer.** probe7
  (vendored upstream CoolingBuffer, Ginger config) on ares's
  pre-cooling layer-0 produces F841 — identical to ares. The
  pre-cooling move set is byte-identical to oracle's emitted moves
  (only M73/F differ downstream). So the oracle's *real* CoolingBuffer
  must see a different layer input — most likely a line the oracle
  counts into layer-0 `time_total` that ares excludes (or vice versa),
  worth ~0.34s of layer time. Candidate: an `adjustable`/`time_max`
  marking difference on a boundary line (external perimeter or the
  speed-modifier aggregation), not resolvable from the post-cooling
  bytes alone.

Remaining unverified lead: diff the cooling `adjustable()` marking —
`dont_slow_down_outer_wall` and the `_EXTRUDE_SET_SPEED` speed-modifier
aggregation for the prime/unretract (`G1 E55`) and Z-hop lines may mark
one extra line adjustable in oracle's build.

SV06 0.4 High-Speed is a separate single-coordinate ULP: `Y98.188` vs
`Y98.189` on an outer-wall vertex (precise_outer_wall=1,
resolution=0.012) — a 1-Coord-unit perimeter intersection difference.

## M73 placement + remaining-divergence root causes (2026-09-21 cont)

Multi-run racebatch on the M73 group revealed the stale summary was
misleading: **Qidi X-Max 3 0.4 matched 2/4 fresh oracle runs** (the
M73 placement is oracle-non-deterministic — TBB reordering moves the
marker between the crossing move and the next). So X-Max 3 is
effectively converged under the multi-run rule, not a real bug.

Upstream M73 emission (`GCodeProcessor.cpp:1455-1467`): for each
emitted G1 it writes the line then calls `process_line_move(
g1_lines_counter++)`; the `g1_times_cache` stores `{g1_line_id,
remaining_internal_g1_lines, cumulative_time}` per motion block where
`g1_line_id` is pre-incremented (`++m_g1_line_id` at :3868/:4278 before
`block.g1_line_id = m_g1_line_id`). Ares mirrors this with
`elapsed[index+1]` keyed on a sequential `exported_g1_lines` id — the
schemes align, so residual M73 shifts are boundary-line races or a
block-classification count difference (G28/M83/toolchange g1 ids).

**TinyBotV1 0.4mm (0/4)** is a real divergence, not a race: the
post-retract travel picks a different avoid-crossing waypoint
(oracle `X105.515 Y101.24` two-hop vs ares `X103.821 Y103.928`), and
the inner-wall loop's closing vertex differs (`107.847,102.113` vs
`107.887,107.847`) — a seam/loop-orientation + avoid-crossing routing
difference, same family as Sermoon V1's known avoid-crossing detour.

Net: the genuinely-open defects reduce to (a) avoid-crossing travel
routing (Sermoon V1, TinyBotV1, SK1 wipe-reorder, PhoenixProV1, iQ
TiQ8), (b) sub-ULP perimeter-vertex geometry (SV06 `Y98.188/.189`,
Kobra 3, E3NG, Delta Flyer), (c) cooling-buffer layer-input marking
~0.28s (Ginger G1 feedrate + its M73/time tail), and (d) a residual
M73 off-by-one that may partly be oracle race noise (X-Max3 proved
2/4). Items (a)/(b) are the same deep-geometry class the ledger already
flags as needing oracle-internal frame introspection.

### TinyBotV1 seam-classification detail

TinyBotV1 uses `seam_position=aligned_back` → `spAlignedBack`. Ares
ports it (`visibility.rs:34` `front_adjustment` mirrors the
`clamp((normal·(0,-1,0)+1.2)*0.5)` bias; `alignment.rs:is_better`
mirrors `SeamComparator` overhang→embedded→score ordering). The
divergence is which of two *symmetric back corners* of the cube wins:
oracle picks `(107.887,102.113)`, ares picks `(107.887,107.887)`. Both
satisfy "back"; the tie is broken by the per-vertex `score` (angle
penalty + central_enforcer + accumulated visibility). A symmetric-cube
corner tie-break that differs means a sub-ULP difference in the angle
penalty or a `central_enforcer` flag — same deep-geometry class as the
sub-ULP perimeter-vertex group. Not a missing feature; a precision
frontier inside an already-ported subsystem.

## Option-coverage harness: provably-legal gate (2026-09-21)

Audited the option-coverage suite (the goal's bool/enum/range
contract). Found and fixed two coverage-model gaps:

1. `coFloatOrPercent` `*_line_width` options emitted a raw-schema `max`
   case (`min(max=1000, max_literal=10) = 10`, rendered `10%`) that the
   oracle rejects — the slicing-feasible bound is the nozzle-relative
   context max (`Print::validate`), far below both schema bounds.
   Routed all 9 width options (`line_width`, `bridge_line_width`,
   `outer/inner_wall`, `initial_layer`, `sparse_infill`,
   `internal_solid_infill`, `support`, `top_surface`) through
   `widths::generate`'s context-bound model (mm `2.0` / `500%`).
2. Replaced the `width_domain.is_some()` PASS gate with a
   `provably_legal` flag on `OptionPlan`: bool/enum exhaust their
   declared value set, `min`+`max`-bounded numerics cover the schema
   range, and `min`-only-bounded numerics (`[min,∞)` — e.g.
   `outer_wall_speed min=1`, `travel_speed`, `layer_height`) now
   generate `min`+`seeded` (previously baseline-only). Provably-legal
   coverage went 348→519 of 650. The remaining 131 are genuinely
   unbounded (coString/coPoints/gcode blobs/symbolic bounds like
   `min=-max_temp`) — correctly baseline-only; no schema min/max exists
   to exercise.

Commits `5ff8ac47`, `6675d82e`. The reclassified options now actually
execute against the oracle under `ARES_PARITY_OPTIONS=1`; the residual
FAILs are real feature-parity gaps (gcode_comments inline comments,
nozzle_volume_type flush matrix serialization, print_flow_ratio stats
label, raft_layers max_z_height, sparse_infill_pattern 1-ulp E) plus
M73-placement oracle races.

### raft_layers real divergence (post width-fix re-run)

`raft_layers=100` now slices on both sides (the earlier "max_z 10 vs
40" was a stale oracle-error artifact). Real residual diffs:
- `max_z_height 40.08` vs `40.10` (raft top 0.02mm).
- Raft-start fan/placeholder: oracle emits `; Filament gcode\nM106 S0`
  (the `filament_start_gcode` template body) before the first raft
  layer; ares emits bare `M106 S255` — the template emission ordering
  at the raft/first-layer boundary differs.
- Raft outline geometry: oracle's first raft move `X95.696 Y95.696`
  vs ares `X90.938 Y91.062` — the raft expansion polygon differs,
  cascading into all raft infill coords (~10k diff lines).
Z-spacing matches (0.32/0.62/0.92); the divergence is raft polygon
expansion + first-layer template/fan ordering, not layer height.

### gcode_comments scope

`gcode_comments=1` decorates ~3000 lines across every role (infill,
perimeter, retract, fan, temp, custom gcode) via comment params threaded
through `GCodeWriter::_travel_to_z`/`extrude`/`move`. Ares emits some
(`move to first X point`, `normal lift Z` partially wired in
`approach.rs`/`restate.rs`) but `lift.rs:emit_pending` `LiftMode::*`
emit bare `G1 Z` without ` ; normal lift Z`/` ; spiral lift Z`, and the
per-role extrusion comments (`; infill`, `; perimeter`) are not
emitted. Full parity = threading a description string through the
whole emit surface — a large feature port for an off-by-default option.

### print_flow_ratio footer is a misalignment, not a label bug

Re-ran the case: ares's footer order matches oracle exactly
(`[mm],[cm3],[g],[cost],total[g],total[cost],layers,time`). The
reported `"; total filament used [g]" vs "; filament used [g]"` diff at
byte 79889 is a diff-alignment artifact — ares emits one extra/one
fewer line earlier in the file (per-extruder vs total block offset),
so the comparator paired the wrong labels. The real divergence is a
line-count delta upstream of the footer, not a missing "total" prefix.

Confirmed `print_flow_ratio`, `gcode_comments`, `raft_layers`,
`nozzle_volume_type`, `use_relative_e_distances`, `timelapse_type`,
`printable_height`, and the pattern options are all entangled
feature-parity or oracle-process-failure gaps — none are surgical
footer/label fixes. They each require porting the corresponding
upstream emission block (comment threading, raft polygon expansion,
flush-matrix serialization, absolute-E stats path).

## Option-coverage targeted re-verification + attributable slice rejections (2026-09-21 liii)

Fresh `ARES_OPTION_KEYS`-scoped sweeps (ARES_ORCA_RUNS=8) on the Ender-3
fixture re-classified several earlier FAIL entries that were stale or
oracle-race noise, and surfaced which are genuine gaps:

- **host_type / input_shaping_type / infill_combination / machine_max_jerk_e
  — all PASS now.** Earlier "no match across N runs" verdicts were the M73
  placement race resolving unluckily; on re-run ares byte-matches the common
  oracle ordering (host_type=octoprint matched 3/4 fresh runs, diff=0).
- **bottom_surface_pattern 7/8 PASS** (only `hilbertcurve` DIVERGENT);
  **top_surface_pattern 7/8 PASS** (only `octagramspiral` DIVERGENT) — both
  residual diffs are the wipe-segment sub-ULP E below, not feature gaps.
- **outer_wall_line_width / initial_layer_line_width all PASS** — the
  `widths::generate` context-bounded domain fix holds.
- **use_relative_e_distances/false, printable_height/min — INPUT_REJECTED.**
  Oracle `Print::validate` rejects them: `use_relative_e_distances=0` with a
  `G92 E0` in `before_layer_change_gcode` → -51 "incompatible with absolute
  extruder addressing" (Print.cpp:1740-1746); `printable_height=0` → -51
  "object exceeds the maximum build volume height" (Print.cpp:1389). These
  are schema-legal values that are infeasible on the cube fixture, not
  slicing-parity failures.

Harness changes (commits 3a8bb115, c3a7417b):
- `stages::attributable` now recognizes the two -51 diagnostics above, keyed
  to `use_relative_e_distances` / `printable_height`.
- New `compare_exported_for(key)` classifies a slice-stage attributable
  rejection as an `INPUT_REJECTED` outcome (was ORCA_ERROR — the oracle
  error surfaced inside compare_exported before input_rejection ran);
  execute_plan counts it as a rejected case → domain INCOMPLETE on the
  fixture, which is the honest verdict.
- `ARES_OPTION_KEYS=a,b,c` env narrows the sweep for targeted re-verification.

### sparse_infill_pattern — genuine capability gap

16 of 26 values are `ARES_ERROR`: ares `parse_sparse_infill_pattern`
(patterns.rs:100) returns InvalidInput "not implemented" for
concentric, honeycomb, 3dhoneycomb, gyroid, tpmsd, tpmsfk, lightning,
lateral-*, alignedrectilinear, archimedeanchords, hilbertcurve, crosszag,
lockedzag, line, octagramspiral, quartercubic, tri-hexagon. Each is a
separate upstream fill generator port — a large deferred body, not a
surgical fix. `zigzag` (1573-line geometry diff) is a real divergence.

### sub-ULP wipe-segment E (adaptivecubic / supportcubic)

`adaptivecubic`/`supportcubic` differ by ONE line: `G1 X114.02 Y106.369
E-.38885` (oracle) vs `-.38886` (ares) — the second wipe segment's dE.
`Wipe::wipe` (GCode.cpp:~470): `dE = length * (segment_length / wipe_dist)`,
per-segment delta-E. First segment matches (`-.61115`), so `length`/`during`
agrees; the residual is `segment_length` for the clipped segment — the
`clip_end` truncating-cast boundary point or int `Line::length` rounding
differs by 1 ulp. Same class as the SV06 `Y98.188/.189` perimeter sub-ULP;
needs byte-exact `Polyline::clip_end` + `Line::length` f64/int semantics.

### filament_map_mode — config-block echo normalization

`Auto For Match`/`Manual` produce identical G-code (same byte length); the
sole diff is the `; filament_map_mode = ` CONFIG_BLOCK comment: oracle
echoes `Auto For Flush` (the default), ares echoes the input value. For a
single-filament print oracle normalizes the mode back to fmmAutoForFlush.
Comment-only, zero functional impact; the upstream normalization site is in
the 3mf→Print config path, not yet pinpointed.

## raft_layers — fan/preamble/Z schedule fixed; polygon outline remains (2026-09-21 liv)

Three real `raft_layers` bugs fixed (commits 123f0022, c2dc8640):

- **filament-start gcode + fan at raft base.** The schedule's first entry
  is a raft chunk carrying `RAFT_SENTINEL_BASE + raft_index`, so the print
  preamble (`filament_start_gcode`) deferred to the first OBJECT layer and
  `cooling.begin_layer` saw a u32::MAX layer index → part fan emitted
  `M106 S255` instead of `M106 S0` (close_fan_the_first_x_layers). Fix:
  the preamble fires on `first_group` (first PHYSICAL chunk), and the
  cooling layer index maps raft sentinels → raft index, object layers →
  `layer_index + raft_count`. Oracle now matches: `; filament start gcode`
  + `M106 S0` at raft base, `M106 S255` at the raft→object boundary.
- **gap_raft_object honors independent_support_layer_height.** Upstream
  Slicing.cpp:153-160 only rounds the raft→object gap to a layer_height
  multiple when `independent_support_layer_height` is OFF; with it on
  (the KSR profile sets '1') the gap stays the raw raft_contact_distance.
  Both `slicing_parameters` and the SliceOptions raft path rounded
  unconditionally → object first layer at Z0.9 instead of 0.8 and
  max_z_height 10.70 vs 10.60. Fixed in both paths.

After these, the raft Z schedule (0.2/0.5/0.8/1.0), max_z_height, fan
sequence, and filament-start placement all match the oracle.

### remaining: raft base polygon outline geometry

The raft base flange outline diverges (~2000 cascading lines): oracle
produces a chamfered square (single `jtSquare`/`jtMiter` corner vertex,
`X118.941→117.892`), ares produces a larger, rounded-arc outline with
4-5 corner segments (`X119.361→119.1→118.326→117.698`, extends to 119.36
vs 118.94). The `raft_expansion` jtMiter offset is faithful
(DefaultJoinType=miter, limit=3); the divergence is in the
`SupportGridPattern::extract_support` contact silhouette or the
interface/first-layer `expand_square` chain producing rounded joins and a
larger expansion. Needs the upstream SupportMaterial raft-base polygon
chain traced against the ares offset path — deferred as a deep clipper
boundary, not a join-type slip.

## Option-sweep real-FAIL fixes + harness merge (2026-09-21 liv)

Re-ran the full option sweep (452/650 bounded domains complete, up from ~390).
Of the 198 non-PASS rows: ~150 are INCOMPLETE multi-value/string domains that
never got provably-legal coverage; ~39 FAILs are the recurring M73 race
(`M73 P10 R7` vs `R8`) rather than real divergences. Three real fixes landed:

- `default_jerk` seeded case: `M205 X1.9624` was emitted `X1.962`. Upstream
  `GCodeWriter::set_jerk_xy` (GCodeWriter.cpp:295) writes jerk via ostream
  `<<` (6 significant figures), not the 3-decimal axis quantizer. Added
  `format::ostream` (6-sig-fig, trailing-zero-trimmed) for M205 X/Y. PASS.
- `gcode_comments` lift comments: `; normal lift Z` trails the normal and
  slope Z-lift lines; `;spiral lift Z` is a standalone line before the spiral
  feedrate; arc-fitting spiral uses `G17 ; XY plane for arc`. Applied to
  emit_pending/append_eager/append_spiral. Partial — role comments and the
  move-to-first Z-drop placement still differ.
- `; printing object` label ordering: ares wrote it at object entry before
  the retract; upstream queues it (set_object_start_str) and flushes inside
  travel_to between the retract/lift and the XY move (GCode.cpp:7467). Now
  queued in pending_object_comment and emitted by append_object_start.
  Fixes the enable_wrapping_detection label misplacement.

Harness: write_summary now merges filtered ARES_OPTION_KEYS results over the
prior summary (previously a targeted run clobbered the full-sweep baseline).

Remaining real divergences after this batch: travel `G1 X..Y..Z..` vs bare
`G1 X..Y..` (deferred-lift Z re-statement timing under wrapping detection),
bottom/top_surface_pattern hilbert/octagramspiral fill geometry, cooling
F2001-vs-F2005 feedrate cap, wipe-segment E sub-ULP, 16 unimplemented sparse
fill patterns, and the ~150 INCOMPLETE multi-value/string domains awaiting
provably-legal coverage.

## nozzle_volume_type + scalar-array tolerance (2026-09-22 lviii)

- `nozzle_volume_type` non-Standard on a single-filament print now collapses
  `flush_volumes_matrix` to `[0]` — upstream recomputes the matrix and resizes
  it to `filament_count^2 * extruder_count` zeros when `nozzle_volume_type`
  changes (OrcaSlicer.cpp:3351 trigger, :3429 resize). Byte-identical for
  `nozzle_volume_type=High Flow`.
- `print_flow_ratio` (and every scalar `OrcaFloat`/`Millimeters`) now tolerates
  the GUI's single-element-array storage (`["0.9"]`) — `FloatVisitor::visit_seq`
  unwraps element 0, matching `ConfigOptionFloat::deserialize`. Ares previously
  rejected sequences; Orca accepts and uses the element. Empty arrays still
  reject (matches upstream). This unblocks the whole scalar-as-array sweep class.

## Scalar-as-array tolerance broadened (2026-09-22 lix)

Orca's `ConfigBase::load_from_json` flattens a JSON array value to a CSV string
(`parse_str_arr`, Config.cpp:831-866) then `set_deserialize`s it — a scalar
`coFloat`/`coInt`/`coBool`/`coPercent`/`coFloatOrPercent` therefore accepts
`["0.9"]` (and even `["0.8","0.7"]`, parsing element 0). Ares previously
rejected any sequence for scalar fields. `visit_seq` added to `OrcaFloat`
(→ `Millimeters`), `Percent`, `FloatOrPercent`, `OrcaBool`, and the integer
visitor: unwrap element 0, drain the rest. Empty arrays still reject via
`invalid_type(Seq)`. Updated the over-strict `project_runtime_options::invalid`
test to use `[{}]` (object element — still rejected upstream, `parse_str_arr`
returns false) instead of `["1"]`. All 1660 options tests pass; this unblocks
every option the sweep injector writes as a scalar array.

## Sweep status after array-tolerance + matrix fixes (2026-09-22 lx)

Bounded-domain verdicts now 471 PASS / 36 FAIL / 143 INCOMPLETE (was
459/52/139). The scalar-as-array fix flipped a broad class of `coFloat`,
`coInt`, `coBool`, `coPercent`, `coFloatOrPercent` options that the GUI (and
sweep injector) store as single-element arrays. The residual FAIL rows that are
NOT the M73 ordering class reduce to: `sparse_infill_pattern`/`bottom_surface_pattern`/
`top_surface_pattern`/`support_base_pattern` unimplemented Fill*.cpp generators
(archimedeanchords, octagramspiral, concentric, honeycomb, 3dhoneycomb, gyroid,
tpmsd, tpmsfk, lightning, lateral-*, crosszag, lockedzag, line, quartercubic,

`enable_wrapping_detection` label/lift ordering on re-lifting travel;
`filament_map_mode` config-echo normalization; `gcode_comments` partial port.

The dominant residual is the M73 boundary ordering: `M73 P<R>` lands 1-2 G1
lines late because ares's per-block estimated times accumulate marginally
slower than the oracle's `g1_times_cache` boundary chain — the same estimator
precision class already tracked for the cooling ~0.28s divergence. Several
option FAILs (`wipe_tower_filament`, `skin_infill_line_width`, `print_flow_ratio`,
`top_surface_line_width`, `tree_support_brim_width`) are M73-only diffs, not

## sparse_infill_pattern true state (2026-09-22 lxi)

Direct oracle+ares sweep over all 27 `sparse_infill_pattern` values on the
Ender-3 fixture (after the scalar-array fix):

- Byte-identical (diff = generated header only): rectilinear, grid,
  crosshatch, triangles, 3dhoneycomb, gyroid.
- Rejected `UnsupportedProjectFeature` (the `bridge_over_infill`
  `anchor_projection::validate` whitelist gates anchoring, AND
  `sparse_anchoring.rs` has an explicit `unreachable!` arm for the rest):
  alignedrectilinear, line, crosszag, lockedzag, tri-hexagon, quartercubic,
  lightning, honeycomb, lateral-honeycomb, lateral-lattice, tpmsd, tpmsfk,
  concentric, hilbertcurve, archimedeanchords, octagramspiral. Whitelisting
  alone is NOT sufficient — `generate_sparse_infill_polylines_for_anchoring`
  (`sparse_anchoring.rs:262` `unreachable!`) needs a per-pattern anchoring
  generator; each also needs the `Fill*.cpp` generator itself where absent.
- Real content divergence (slices but feedrate/E differs): zigzag, cubic,
  adaptivecubic, supportcubic — all trace to the cooling/estimator feedrate
  chain (sorted-content diff shows `G1 F943` count divergence), the same
  documented estimator-precision class as the M73 ordering.
- `monotonic` sparse: oracle emits nothing for this model (no output file),
  ares rejects — both fail; excluded.

Confirmed the M73 placement is a genuine oracle TBB race: two identical oracle
runs emit `M73 P10 R7` at line 143 vs 145; ares matches run2 byte-for-byte.
So M73-gated FAILs are races, not divergences — a re-run can flip them.

The fill-generator + anchoring ports are the dominant real remaining option
work: each rejected pattern needs its `Fill*.cpp` `fill_surface` generator
AND a `sparse_anchoring` arm for the bridge-over-infill path.

oracle M73-placement races (stripped-diff 0), i.e. real PASSes counted as FAIL.

Real content divergences remaining after M73 stripping (stripped-diff >2):
- `raft_layers` seeded/max — the largest class (4 cases ~4600 diff lines each).
  ares `;TYPE:Support` raft regions run ~60-70 lines longer than oracle and the
  per-layer toolpath diverges (`G1 X99.703 Y99.834` vs oracle path); the
  R-value time estimate is off by ~2min. The earlier `raft_layers` fixes
  (preamble on first_group `123f0022`, raw contact distance `c2dc8640`) closed
  the base case but seeded/max raft geometry still diverges.
- A tail of `zigzag`/`cubic`/etc cooling-estimator `F<feedrate>` cases
  (stripped-diff 20-165) — the known f32 `line.time`/`feedrate` accumulation
  class.

`timelapse_type` is the only non-`no match` FAIL (oracle silent -102 /
capability limitation). The dominant real divergence is `raft_layers` — the
raft toolpath generator needs a focused fix pass, not the M73/feedrate class.

### raft_layers divergence detail (lxiii follow-up)

First raft difference is at the contact/support outline, not the infill: oracle
emits `G1 X99.77 Y99.77` (square-ish corner) where ares emits
`G1 X99.703 Y99.834` then a rounded-corner chain
(`X100.432 Y99.18` → `X101.058 Y98.809` → …). The raft base polygon corner
treatment differs — likely a different offset/join type or expansion radius in
the raft outline generator (`project_slice/raft/`). Each `;TYPE:Support` raft
section runs ~60-70 lines longer in ares. Fix the raft outline corner/offset
first; the R-value 2-min overestimate is downstream of the longer toolpath.

#### raft skirt start vertex (lxiii follow-up 2)

The first `raft_layers` divergence is the **skirt start vertex**, not the raft
interior: oracle moves to `G1 X99.77 Y99.77`, ares to `G1 X99.703 Y99.834`
(~0.09mm off). The skirt outline is `convex_hull(occupied)` where `occupied`
includes raft fill points when `raft_layers>0` (`gcode_emit/skirt.rs:97-125`),
then `find_start_point`/`skirt_start_angle` picks the start. The hull or the
start-point pick differs because the raft polygon corners feeding the hull are
slightly different. Also ares drops the `M73 P2 R30` that oracle emits after
`G1 Z.6`/`G1 Z.2` before `;TYPE:Skirt` — a separate M73 boundary placement on
the raft layer. Trace `raft::bridge::build_project_raft` fill points vs the
oracle raft outline to find the corner delta.

#### raft contact outline corner shape (lxiii follow-up 3)

Root cause narrowed further: the raft **contact outline** (`SupportGridPattern::
extract_support` → `EdgeGrid::contours_simplified` marching-squares SDF) emits
**rounded/octagonal** corners (ares `X100.639…X119.361`, multi-step) while oracle
emits a **chamfered rectangle** (`X101.059…X118.941`, single 45° corner cut).
ares's contact is ~0.42mm larger AND rounded. `RAFTPOLY` debug shows ares
contact half-extent ±7150601 (7.15mm) — but oracle contact is ~±8.94mm, so the
ares raft contact is actually *smaller* than oracle in X-extent while the gcode
outline extends further — the discrepancy is the corner geometry, not the
extent: oracle chamfers the corner (removes the rounded bulge), ares keeps a
rounded outline that pokes past the chamfer line. The `extract_support` SDF
corner-shrink / marching-squares corner pick (`edge_grid/simplify.rs`,
`EdgeGrid.cpp:1283-1400`) or the `raft_expansion` `expand` join path needs an
oracle contact-polygon dump to align.

#### raft contact extract offset ruled out (lxiii follow-up 4)

Swapping the raft `extract_support` offset from `expansion_to_slice` to
`expansion_to_propagate` (-3) made the diff *worse* (17465 vs ~4600 stripped
lines), so `expansion_to_slice` is correct for the contact silhouette. The
rounded-vs-chamfered corner difference is therefore inside
`EdgeGrid::contours_simplified` marching-squares corner handling or the
`contacts_input` = `expand(overhangs, raft_expansion, jtMiter, 3.0)` step — not
the extract offset. Needs an oracle `contact_polygons`/`extract_support` dump
to diff the raw contour; add a nix probe in `SupportMaterial.cpp` around
`detect_contacts` layer 0.

#### raft grid resolution (lxiii follow-up 5)

`RAFTDBG` shows ares contact `contour 0 inner=false pts=16` (octagonal/rounded,
16 pts) where oracle emits an 8-vertex chamfered rectangle. The contact outline
comes from `SupportGridPattern::extract_support` → `EdgeGrid::contours_simplified`
at `grid_resolution = scale_(support_base_pattern_spacing(2.5) +
support_flow_spacing)` where `support_flow_spacing = support_width - layer_height
*(1-π/4)`. If ares's `support_width`/`layer_height`/`support_flow_spacing` for the
raft differ from upstream `support_material_flow.spacing()`, the SDF grid
resolution changes → different corner quantization (rounded vs chamfered). Next:
dump upstream `grid_resolution`/`m_pixel_size` for the raft contact (probe at
`SupportMaterial.cpp:620`/`extract_support` `:735`) and the raw `contact_polygons`
to compare the lattice-resolution corner handling vertex-for-vertex.

#### raft contact = AGG rasterizer, not EdgeGrid SDF (lxiv — root cause)

**Confirmed the real divergence**: `SUPPORT_USE_AGG_RASTERIZER` is `#define`d at
`SupportMaterial.cpp:21`, so `extract_support` (`:751-760`) uses the **AGG
scanline-AA rasterizer** path — `rasterize_polygons` → `m_grid2` (gray8 coverage
bytes) → `contours_simplified` (`:204`, the *static* grid-walk version, NOT the
EdgeGrid `m_grid.contours_simplified`) — plus `seed_fill_block` (`:1001`) and
`dilate_trimming_region` (`:980`) macro-block propagation. ares instead ported
`EdgeGrid::contours_simplified` (`edge_grid/simplify.rs`, the `#else` branch),
which produces rounded SDF corners where the AGG path produces grid-aligned
chamfered corners. That is exactly the raft contact outline divergence.

Fix = port the AGG path: `rasterize_polygons` (anti-aliased gray8 coverage —
`!= 0` means any partial coverage), `seed_fill_block`, `dilate_trimming_region`,
the `oversampling` macro-block sizing (`:676-684`), and the simple
`contours_simplified` (`:204-330`). ares has no scanline-AA rasterizer; this is
a real port, and it affects all `extract_support` consumers (raft + support),
not just raft. See `fill`/`support_grid` for the seam.

#### AGG rasterizer ported; residual = skirt round-join segment count (lxv)

The `extract_support` AGG rasterizer port (`geometry/agg_raster.rs`,
`82ecd1eb`) is a faithful `rasterizer_cells_aa`+`sweep_scanline` port —
the raft contact polygon is now a clean 4-vertex square `(±7.151)` where the
EdgeGrid SDF produced a 16-vertex rounded outline. The contact polygon is
correct now.

The remaining `raft_layers` byte divergence is the **skirt outline**:
ares `jtRound` offsets the occupied hull to a multi-segment arc corner
(`X120.714 Y100.28`, `X121.364 Y101.987`) while oracle emits a single 45°
chamfer (`X120.755 Y100.294` → `X121.506 Y102.108`). Both use `jtRound` +
`scale_(0.1)` arc tolerance (`Print.cpp:2736` vs `skirt.rs:192`). The
divergence is the round-join segment count for the hull corner — likely
`do_round` step count (`fixed_round(steps_per_radian * angle)`) or the
occupied hull's corner vertex differing (raft first_layer vs oracle's
`convex_hull(group.points)` input). Needs a `DoRound` steps comparison.

#### AGG rasterizer verified — raft_layers/min byte-identical (lxvi)

`raft_layers/min` (`case-j3o1ut`) now diffs to **0 lines** after the AGG
rasterizer port — `extract_support` produces the correct contact outline. The
`max`/`seeded` cases (multi-layer rafts) still diverge on the skirt `jtRound`
corner segment count (oracle emits a single 45° chamfer, ares a multi-segment
arc). Both use `jtRound` + `scale_(0.1)`; the divergence is in the occupied
hull input to the skirt offset or the round-step count for the hull corner.

`agg_raster` split into `mod/cells/grid_ops/contours` (all <400 LOC). 260
related tests pass, 0 failures.

### lxxxii. raft residual — first_layer asymmetry is upstream input, not AGG (post-d8e98eff)
`upscale`→`iround` (round-half-away, `agg_basics.h:188`) applied — faithful, no
change (subpixel coords here don't hit `.5`). ares `first_layer` is symmetric
