object footprint is symmetric, the asymmetry enters via the AGG `rasterizer_scanline_aa`
scanline AA cell-coverage — oracle's `extract_support` raster of a symmetric polygon
yields a ~1px asymmetric `contact`. ares's `agg_raster` `line()`/`sweep_scanline`
cover/area accumulation is symmetric where oracle's is not. Residual = AGG
scanline AA subpixel coverage asymmetry — the deepest primitive; the raft
connect/offset/clipper layers above it are ALL byte-faithful Clipper1.

### lxxxiii. raft residual — STRUCTURAL: first_layer/base built from support-generator layers, not footprint extract
`enable_support:0`, `raft_layers:100`. ares `contact`/`first_layer` symmetric
(`±7150585`/`±9150585`); oracle `first_layer`/`interface` asymmetric
(`9150587/-9150572`). Root cause is STRUCTURAL, not AGG: upstream
`generate_raft_base` (`SupportCommon.cpp:244-390`) builds raft polygons from the
SUPPORT GENERATOR's layers — `top_contacts`/`interface_layers`/
`base_interface_layers`/`base_layers` (`columns_base`). With raft on, the support
generator produces these even with `enable_support:0`. `interface_polygons =
union(expand(top_contacts,fine), expand(interfaces,fine), expand(base_interfaces,fine))`
(`:302-311`); `first_layer = union(columns_base.polygons, union(base, interface_polygons))`
(`:347`). The asymmetry is the support-column contact region, NOT `extract_support`.
ares `raft_polygons` fabricates `contact`/`interface`/`base`/`first_layer` from
`extract_support(first_layer_lslices)` — a symmetric footprint raster — and has NO
`top_contacts`/`base_layers`/`interfaces`/`columns_base` inputs. Closing this needs
the support-column layer generation (detect_contacts + propagate + support layers)
fed into `raft_polygons` — a substantive `SupportMaterial`/`SupportCommon` port
slice, not a quick fix.

### lxxxiv. config-echo `sparse_infill_pattern` canonical value + zigzag connect residual
`case-rubl2p`/`8AsS4U`/`03RaeG` (sparse_infill_pattern=zigzag): ares echoed
`sparse_infill_pattern = zig-zag` (the serde `rename` on `enums.rs:167`); Orca's
canonical enum label is `zigzag` (`PrintConfig.cpp:3024,7770`; `zig-zag` is a
legacy input alias `:8206`). Fixed `rename = "zigzag", alias = "zig-zag"` —
serialize canonical, accept both. Residual (~946→944): the `internal_solid_infill`
monotonic serpentine on odd layers emits the zigzag connect rotated 90°
(`X113.656 Y114.02` horizontal vs `X114.02 Y113.656` vertical) — a
`map_infill_end_point_to_boundary`/`take_next` traversal-direction divergence in
`connect_infill`, not the zigzag link logic (`link_zigzag` index-parity is faithful
`FillBase.cpp:2584-2596`) and not `_layer_angle` (`FillZigZag` doesn't override →
alternates 90°, ares `keeps_layer_angle_fixed=false` correct). Also a 1-line
`estimated printing time` diff (8m55s vs 8m54s, estimator rounding) and F943/F944.

### lxxxv. zigzag sparse serpentine transpose — connect_base_support chain direction
`case-rubl2p`/`8AsS4U` `;TYPE:Sparse infill` (zigzag 15%): the same 9-segment
zigzag emitted rotated 90° — oracle `X113.656 Y114.02→X109.818` (top-edge
horizontal start) vs ares `X114.02 Y113.656→X114.02 Y109.818` (right-edge vertical
start). Fill lines match (`X114.02 Y*`); the `connect_base_support` chain emission
order/direction transposes the serpentine. `connect.rs` phase order is faithful
(`:24-120` mirrors `FillBase.cpp:2281-2697`); residual is in `take_next`/`take`/
`link_zigzag` chain traversal direction OR `build_working_graph`/
`map_infill_end_point_to_boundary` ordering. Same connect-depth class as the raft
residual — the emit direction of the merged zigzag chains.

### lxxxvi. narrow-region infill — split_solid_surface line-based detection unported
`case-OxtTzz`/`JBIbxQ`/`uJRapD` (crosshatch + monotonic internal solid): ares emits
extra `WIDTH:0.405676` tiny infill segments (`X113.488 Y113.533`/`X113.558 Y113.568`,
~E.0023) oracle skips. Upstream `detect_narrow_internal_solid_infill` →
`split_solid_surface` (`Fill.cpp:597-814`): for LINE-based patterns
(rectilinear/monotonic/monotonicline/alignedrectilinear) it runs fill-line analysis
(`connect_extrusions`, `base_angle = fill.params.angle + M_PI/2`, `:628+`) to split
`narrow_infill`/`normal_infill` geometrically; non-line patterns use a `opening`
core-erode (`:605-626`). ares `concentric_internal_override`/`all_narrow_rectangle_bounds`
(`infills/narrow_internal.rs`) is a single-rectangle-bounds shortcut — diverges on
any non-rectangle narrow region. Also produces extra `; infill`/`; move to first
infill point` comments. Port `split_solid_surface` line-based detection.

### lxxxvii. narrow-internal concentric override misfires (detect_narrow default on)
`detect_narrow_internal_solid_infill:1` is DEFAULT — present in all divergent
cases. ares `concentric_internal_override` (`infills.rs:298` → `narrow_internal.rs`)
fires `ConcentricInternal` via `all_narrow_rectangle_bounds` (single 4-pt-rectangle
contour, `min(w,h) <= 2·solid_line_width`), emitting `WIDTH:0.405676` concentric
slivers oracle doesn't (`OxtTzz`: oracle 2 occurrences vs ares 4 — extra on
non-narrow layers). Upstream `split_solid_surface` (`Fill.cpp:597`) for LINE
patterns (monotonic/rectilinear) computes `normal_fill_areas` via vertical-section
analysis + `opening` core-erode — a region a whole-level rectangle-bounds test
can't reproduce. `concentric_internal_override` misfires on corner slivers that
are rectangles-but-not-narrow. Fix = port `split_solid_surface` line-based path
(vertical_lines sections → `filter_vibrating_extrusions` → `TracedPoly` reconstruct
→ `union_safety_offset_ex`/`diff_ex` split → touch-merge) into `detect_narrow`.

### lxxxviii. correction — split_solid_surface IS ported (group_fills/narrow/split.rs)
Correction to lxxxvi/lxxxvii: `split_solid_surface` IS ported as
`group_fills/narrow/split.rs` — `split_lines` is a faithful line-based port
(`opening(2·spacing,3·spacing)` + `shrink(spacing·0.5−overlap)` + `LineDistanceTree`
vertical sections + `filter::apply`(filter_vibrating_extrusions) +
`trace::reconstruct`(TracedPoly) + `union_safety_offset_ex`/`diff_ex` + touch-merge).
The residual is a SUBTLE reconstruction divergence — `intersections_sorted`/`outside`/
`filter`/`trace` producing an extra narrow sliver on some layers — not a missing port.
Verified faithful: `scaled` truncating (`scale_`/`scaled<coord_t>`), `base_angle`
(`+π/2` + odd-layer `+π/2` unless AlignedRectilinear), `aligning_angle`, section
`outside<0 && |Δy|>spacing`, touch-merge thresholds (`-0.5·spacing` shrink /
`+0.3·spacing` expand), final `expand(+0.5·spacing) ∩ fill.expolygons`.

### lxxxix. zigzag serpentine transpose = has_consistent_pattern not threaded (FIXED)
`FillZigZag`/`FillCrossZag`/`FillLockedZag` override `has_consistent_pattern()=true`
(`FillRectilinear.hpp:198,207,219`); `fill_surface_by_lines` passes it to
`traverse_graph_generate_polylines` (`FillRectilinear.cpp:2874`) which alternates
the per-line serpentine direction (`next_start` `i_vline2 & 1`, `connect_horizontal`
left-only, `:1401-1489`) AND anchors the fill bbox on `extended_object_bounding_box`
(`:2785`). ares hard-coded `traverse::generate(slice, false)` — no alternation →
the zigzag serpentine emitted rotated 90°. Fix: `ProcessInfillPattern::
has_consistent_pattern()` (`enums.rs`) threaded into `MonotonicFillParams::
consistent_pattern` → `traverse::generate`. `case-rubl2p` 946→28, `8AsS4U`
1004→90. Residual: a z=9 sparse-layer transpose + `WIDTH:0.405678` narrow sliver +
F2005 cooling (separate classes). `OxtTzz`/`JBIbxQ` (crosshatch) unaffected.

### xc. crosshatch/cooling residual classes isolated (post-zigzag-fix)
After the `has_consistent_pattern` fix (`20e25676`), the dominant sweep divergence
is **crosshatch** (48+ of first 50 divergent cases; `case-OxtTzz`/`JBIbxQ`/`uJRapD`).
Stripping comments, the crosshatch diff is almost entirely **cooling feedrate**
(`F2005`/`F2002`, `F1982`/`F1973`, `F1998`, `F3000`) — the crosshatch FILL geometry
matches byte-for-byte; the residual is `CoolingBuffer::adjust`'s `new_feedrate`.
`slowdown.rs`/`measure_movement`/`parse.rs` verified faithful (`line.time =
length/feedrate`, f32 `F/60`, `elapsed_time`/`maximum_time` pre-sort, gcc-stable
adjustable-first+feedrate-desc sort, `new_feedrate_to_reach_time_stretch` 20-iter
double nomin/denom). Residual = a per-line `time`/`adjustable` input diff needing
an oracle `CoolingBuffer` line-dump probe. Secondary: ares over-emits `; move to
first X point` travel comments under `gcode_comments=1` (only 2 sweep cases; oracle
emits bare travel). `lockedzag`/`crosszag` are honest INPUT_REJECTED (unported).

### xci. cooling F-divergence root-caused to ConcentricInternal medial width (NOT the solver)
Oracle CoolingBuffer probes (coolprobe{,2,3}.nix dumping LOOP/ADJ/SDTF/NFR/LAYER/BRANCH):
`new_feedrate_to_reach_time_stretch` nomin/denom/nfr, LOOP fr/nextfr/stretch, SDTF limits,
and per-layer `total`/`max`/`nlines`/`BRANCH` are BYTE-IDENTICAL between ares and oracle.
The `F2005`/`F2002`/`F1998`/`F2001`/`F1973` diffs are a KNOCK-ON of extra gcode lines:
ares emits ~18 `;WIDTH:0.405676/0.405678/0.429544/0.429546` narrow-infill blocks vs oracle ~6,
which shifts the cooling `lines`/`total`/`stretch` → different solver output. The narrow
regions themselves match (oracle `OFINAL normal=1 narrow=2` == ares `SPLITOUT/FINAL`);
the divergence is `ConcentricInternal`/`generate_thick_polylines` (Arachne WallToolPaths)
emitting 2-point slivers at slightly-different medial widths (0.405676 vs 0.405678, 2nm) and
on layers oracle emits normal-width. The medial-axis bead-width f32/segmentation detail is
the next port-verify slice — split/is_valid/clip_end are all confirmed faithful.

### xcii. narrow-region geometry confirmed byte-exact — divergence is Arachne medial width
Oracle `splitprobe2.nix` `OFINAL` dump proves `split_solid_surface` produces IDENTICAL
regions: oracle `normal=1 narrow=2` on 9 layers + `narrow=1` on 1 == ares `SPLITOUT
normal=1 FINAL narrow=2`/`narrow=1`. The diff is the FILL of those regions: ares's
`ConcentricInternal`/`generate_thick_polylines` (Arachne `WallToolPaths`) emits the
corner sliver `X113.488→X113.558` at `WIDTH:0.405676` on the BOTTOM layers (Z=0.4)
where oracle emits a normal `WIDTH:0.449999` diagonal covering the same area. Both
have `narrow=2`; the Arachne medial bead-width on the narrow corner triangle yields a
`0.405676` sliver in ares vs a `0.45`-width normal-width loop in oracle — a
WallToolPaths width/segmentation precision diff, not a split or is_valid/clip_end bug.
This is the deepest remaining per-layer divergence (drives the WIDTH-count + cooling-F
knock-on across crosshatch/zigzag/monotonic cases). Requires an Arachne
`WallToolPaths`/`ExtrusionLine` width probe — `width` is `coordf_t` (f64) upstream.

### xciii. Arachne medial junction width: oracle 407079 vs ares 362756 (dist2boundary/bead)
`splitprobe2`/`arachneprobe` prove the `split_solid_surface` narrow regions are
BYTE-IDENTICAL (`ONAR`==`FNARROW` corner triangles). The output `;WIDTH:0.405676`
=`unscale(medial_w)+height*(1-π/4)` (`VariableWidth.cpp:38` `with_width`), so
medial `w`=362756↔0.405676. Oracle `OTHICK` junction width = 407079 (≈optimal_width
=spacing 0.407) — oracle's `SkeletalTrapezoidation` assigns the narrow corner's
junction `w`=optimal_width; ares assigns 362756 (=region thickness via
`WideningBeadingStrategy::compute` returning `thickness.max(min_output_width)`).
Upstream `WideningBeadingStrategy::compute` (`WideningBeadingStrategy.cpp`) also
returns `thickness` — so the divergence is NOT beading `compute` but the node
`dist2boundary`/`radius`/`bead_count` feeding it: oracle's medial node `radius·2`
=407079 (a `dist2boundary` reflecting optimal_width) vs ares's =362756. Next probe:
`SkeletalTrapezoidation` `node.data.distance_to_boundary`/`bead_count` and
`get_or_create_beading` `optimal_bead_count(distance*2)` / `compute(radius*2,count)`
— the medial node's radius differs, so the bead width differs.

### xciv. Arachne junction width → dist2boundary node radius (voronoi medial placement)
`distance_to_boundary` is the medial node's perpendicular distance to its source
boundary segment — set in `SkeletalTrapezoidationGraph::makeRib` (`:326`,
`dist = (prev_edge->to->p - p).norm()` where `p` = `distance_to_infinite_squared`
projection onto `Line(start_source,end_source)`) and `insertRib` (`:365`). ares
`operations.rs` `make_rib`/`insert_rib`/`project`/`point_distance` verified faithful.
Oracle junction `w=407079` ↔ `dist2boundary=203540` (≈optimal_width/2); ares
`w=362756` ↔ `dist2boundary=181378`. So the divergence is upstream of beading: the
voronoi medial NODE POSITION/`distance_to_boundary` differs — `SkeletalTrapezoidation::
discretize`/`transferEdge`/`makeNode` (boost::polygon::voronoi → medial vertices)
produces a node at a different radius for the narrow corner region. Next probe:
dump `node.p`/`distance_to_boundary` per voronoi vertex for the narrow region and
compare to ares's skeletal graph — isolates whether the voronoi vertex set or the
discretization/bead_count differs.

### xcv. Junction width = store_node_beadings bead_widths, not get_or_create_beading
`update_bead_count` `ONODE` dist values are byte-identical oracle↔ares
(152660/153815/153817) — medial `distance_to_boundary` for central nodes is correct.
`OBEAD` (in `get_or_create_beading`) never fires: upward-edge `to` nodes hit the
`beading()` early-return because `store_node_beadings` (`segments.rs:31`, upstream
`storeNodeBeadings` `:1470`) already set `beading` = `compute(dist·2, bc)` /
`interpolate_beading(lower,1-ratio,higher)`. ares `OSTORE` dumps:
- `dist=588458 bc=3 w=[407079, 362758, 407079]` — middle bead 362758/362756
- `dist=193313 bc=1 w=[386626]`, `dist=178852 bc=1 w=[357704]`, `dist=152660 bc=1 w=[340000]`
The `;WIDTH:0.405676`↔`w=362756` is the MIDDLE bead of the 3-bead `dist=588458` node;
oracle junction `w=407079` is the OUTER bead (`bead_widths[0]`/`[2]`). So the
divergence is `junction_index`/`toolpath_locations` selection in
`junctions.rs:224-265` (`generateEdgeBeading`), not the beading values — ares picks
the middle bead (362756) where oracle picks the outer (407079). Awaiting storeprobe
oracle OSTORE to confirm bc/dist parity, then compare junction_index selection.

### xcvi. OSTORE beading byte-identical — divergence is segment/polyline assembly
storeprobe oracle `OSTORE` == ares `OSTORE` byte-for-byte: `dist=588458 bc=3
w=[407079,362758,407079]`, `193313→386626`, `178852→357704`, `152660→340000`,
`153817→340000` — `beading`/`compute`/`interpolate_beading`/`junction_index` all
correct. So `bead_widths` are identical; the extra `;WIDTH:0.405676/0.429544`
extrusions are a `generateSegments`/`to_thick_polyline` ASSEMBLY divergence: which
junctions connect into which `ExtrusionLine`, upward/downward edge traversal, or
`connectJunctions`/`connectLines`/`addToPolyline`. Next: compare the ExtrusionLine
count/segment connectivity, not the widths.

### xcvii. ExtrusionLine COUNTS differ — generateSegments edge-grouping divergence
ARESCI vs OTHICK on case-OxtTzz (identical bead_widths, OSTORE byte-match):
| width/closed | ares | oracle |
| 362758 open n=4 | 5 | 4 |
| 386624 open n=4 | 5 | 3 |
| 386626 open n=4 | 4 | 2 |
| 407079 closed n=4 | 5 | 9 |
| 13-pt loop | 1 | 1 |
ares emits MORE open sliver segments (386624/386626/362758) and FEWER closed
407079 loops. bead_widths are identical, so `generateSegments`' edge→ExtrusionLine
traversal groups edges differently — ares breaks the closed 407079 loops into
extra open sliver segments. Next: `generateSegments`/`walk_edge`/loop-closure.

### xcviii. toolpath_locations/beading fully identical → segment GRAPH traversal diverges
ares `OSTORE loc` for bc=3 node = `[203539, 588458, 973377]` (bead radii); w/loc/dist/bc
all byte-match oracle. `add_toolpath_segment` (`toolpaths.rs`) verified faithful to
upstream `addToolpathSegment` (`:1887-1931`, both `from`-join and `to`-reversal
branches). So the extra `;WIDTH:0.405678/0.429544` open segments are from
`generateSegments`/`connectJunctions`/`connectQuadJunctions` — the quad-start edge
walk (`connectJunctions` `:1934+`, `generateQuadStart`/`generateTransitionEnd`) that
emits different per-edge junction sets / segment groupings, not the beading values.

### xcix. Extra slivers = generate_local_maxima hexagons on nodes oracle filters
`OSEG` (add_toolpath_segment) shows only w∈{340000,357704,407079} — the extra
`;WIDTH:0.405676/0.429544` (w=362756/386624/386626) come from
`generate_local_maxima_single_beads` (`local_maxima.rs`, upstream
`generateLocalMaximaSingleBeads` `:2056`): bc=3 middle bead 362758/362756 and

362758×4/386624×3/386626×2 — ares emits extra hexagons for bc=1/bc=3 nodes.
`generate_local_maxima` + `node_is_central` + `node_is_local_maximum` all verified
faithful (incl `!isCentral` check and `canGoUp(strict)`). So the skeletal GRAPH has
extra local-max nodes — a trapezoidation construction divergence upstream of
beading (update_is_central / filterCentral / generateTransitionEnd / isEndOfCentral
edge flags, or voronoi node set). Next: dump per-node isLocalMaximum/isCentral.

### c. Single force_new_path divergence → quad domain boundary
`OSEG` (addToolpathSegment) oracle↔ares are byte-identical except ONE segment:
`f=3942611,-3942607,357704 t=3816144,-3968799,340000 odd=1` — ares `fnew=1 nlines=0`
(domain start, empty lines[inset]), oracle `fnew=0` (appended to existing line).
So `bead_widths`/`dist`/`junction points`/`is_odd`/`3way` ALL match; the divergence
is the `connectJunctions` domain walk — `quad_start`/`getNextUnconnected`/`next->twin`
quad chaining produces a different domain boundary, so ares starts a new ExtrusionLine
where oracle continues the previous one → the extra `;WIDTH` open slivers + cooling-F.
`connect_junction_domain`/`DenseEdgeSet`/`next_unconnected`/`add_toolpath_segment`
verified faithful; the residual is the quad `next`/`prev`/`twin` topology —
`transferEdge`/`makeRib`/`insertRib`/`connectTwins`/`collapseSmallEdges` linking
produces a slightly different quad chain for this corner. NEXT: dump the
quad_start→quad_end→next_unconnected chain per domain to find the topology diff.

### ci. OLMAX emit counts identical — divergence is stitch/merge of odd segments
Oracle `OLMAX emit=1` counts == ares exactly (bc=3 588458×5/588457×4, bc=1
193313×4/193312×5; bc=1 178852 cen=1 emit=0 both). local_maxima hexagons identical.
The `odd=true n=4` sliver lines are the hexagons post-stitch/simplify, OR odd
segments oracle merges into closed loops. The single `OSEG fnew` divergence
(`f=3942611,-3942607,357704 t=3816144,-3968799,340000 odd=1`: ares fnew=1/nlines=0,
oracle fnew=0) → different line split → stitch produces different groupings. Added

### cii. RESOLVED: narrow-region `;WIDTH` divergence = ORACLE NON-DETERMINISM (TBB/hash race)
Root-caused through 6 instrumented oracle builds + ares dumps, all byte-identical:
`ONODE`/`OSTORE`/`bead_widths`/`toolpath_locations`/`OLMAX emit`/`OSEG` — dist, bc,
junction points, widths, segment list all match. The ONLY divergence is the
`connectJunctions` `unprocessed_quad_starts` (`ankerl::unordered_dense::set`)
ITERATION ORDER — a hash/pointer-order race. PROOF: two identical oracle runs of
case-OxtTzz produce different `OQUAD` quad order (64 diff lines) AND different
gcode `;WIDTH` counts: `0.405676` 4/1/4, `0.405678` 3/5/5, `0.429544` 4/5/5.
ares's output (0.405676×4, 0.405678×5, 0.429544×5) falls INSIDE the oracle
variance envelope (matches run1/run3 for 0.405676, run2/run3 for 0.405678/0.429544).
The `detect_narrow`/`ConcentricInternal` narrow slivers + cooling-F knock-on are
NOT a port bug — ares is byte-deterministic within oracle's TBB non-determinism,
same class as the confirmed M73 race. No fix needed; comparator should treat
narrow-infill `;WIDTH`/cooling-F variance as oracle-race noise for detect_narrow cases.

### ciii. Narrow-region gcode is GEOMETRICALLY identical modulo oracle ordering race
ares vs oracle run3 on case-OxtTzz: sorted `E` extrusion amounts diff = 0 (identical
extrusion set); the 164-217 line diff is pure G1/F9000/; reordering of the narrow
`ConcentricInternal` extrusions — the same coordinates/extrusions in a different
line order driven by the `connectJunctions` quad-start hash race. CONFIRMED
non-deterministic: 3 oracle runs give different `;WIDTH` counts and different
OQUAD order. Ares is byte-deterministic and geometrically identical — the residual
`detect_narrow`/crosshatch/zigzag diffs are oracle ordering noise, not a defect.
RESOLUTION: treat `detect_narrow=1` + `ConcentricInternal` narrow-sliver/cooling-F
diffs as oracle-race noise in the comparator; the underlying geometry is proven
byte-equivalent (E-set, node dists, bead widths, junction points all match).

### civ. Sweep FAIL domains are oracle-race noise + stale fixtures, not port bugs
Re-sliced the option-coverage FAIL domains with the CURRENT binary + fresh oracle:
- `gcode_comments/false`, `seam_slope_type/none`, `cool_plate_temp/min`,
  `sparse_infill_pattern/rectilinear`, `top_surface_pattern/archimedeanchords`
  → **now byte-identical (diff=0)** — the summary FAILs were STALE fixtures.
- `activate_air_filtration_during_print`, `bottom_surface_pattern/monotonic` →
  `F2005/F2002` cooling + `;WIDTH` narrow slivers = the CONFIRMED oracle TBB race
  (3 oracle runs: `0.405678` 4/5/4, `0.429546` 4/4/3, `F2005/F2002` 29/30/29 —
  ares matches run2 exactly). Oracle ordering race, not a defect.
- `raft_layers` → 1877 structural diff (real — needs support-column
  top_contacts/columns_base/interface_layers into raft_polygons; the only
  genuine remaining residual of the sampled FAILs).
Of 35 summary FAIL domains, ~18 diverge at `line ~137` (M73 P10 R7 ordering race)
and most others are the narrow-region race or stale — the actionable residual set
is far smaller than the raw FAIL count suggests.

### cv. raft_layers residual decomposed — connect_base_support edge-linking
`raft_layers` (1877-line diff) decomposes into:
- `M73 P3 R26` ordering race (line 67/70) — the known M73 race.
- `E.04556`/`E.04557` — a `fixed_round` boundary rounding (raft outline).
- **Structural**: `connect_base_support` (the `Fill::connect_base_support` /
  `FillRectilinear` port) leaves the leftmost/rightmost fill lines
  (`±8601213` = ±8.6mm) UNCHAINED as `n=2` polylines, so ares emits the
  rightmost edge (`X118.601→117.537 E.46316`) as a separate travel+extrude+wipe
  where oracle chains it into the zigzag. `ARES_CONN_DUMP`: `CONN n=3` =
  left-edge + right-edge + zigzag (should be ONE chain). The `x_margin`
  filter (`make_fill_lines:2924`) drops boundary-adjacent lines, and
  `link_zigzag`/`close_left_caps`/`connect_t_joints`/`emit_very_long_arches`
  do not recover the orphaned edge lines — upstream's connection links them
  via a boundary arch the ares port misses.
The remaining raft `top_contacts`/`columns_base`/`interface_layers` upstream
slice is a separate, still-open structural piece (raft over supported geometry).

### cvi. connect_base_support edge-linking — outermost fill lines orphaned
`ARES_CONN_DUMP` on `raft_layers/seeded` shows the base-layer fill produces
`CONN n=3`: the leftmost (`-8601213`) and rightmost (`+8601213`) vertical fill
lines as standalone `n=2` polylines, plus the `n=88` zigzag. Upstream chains the
edge lines into the zigzag via boundary arches (so the rightmost edge is drawn
continuously, then the zigzag follows). ares's `LinkPhases` pipeline
(`consume_vertical_arches` → `link_zigzag` → `close_left_caps` →
`connect_t_joints` → `emit_very_long_arches`) leaves them unlinked — the
outermost lines' ends either have no `prev`/`next` neighbor on the boundary
side or their arch cost/trim gates reject the link. This is a faithful-structure
port of `Fill::connect_base_support` (`FillRectilinear.cpp:2203-2697`) — the
divergence is in the arch evaluation/link gating for boundary-adjacent lines,
needs a focused `evaluate_support_arches`/`build_working_graph` trace for the
outermost intersections.

### cvii. FIX — emit_loops_in_band finalize erased inner run (raft edge excess)
`BandState::finalize` (`fill/base_support/mod.rs`) skipped the truncate when
`polyline_end == 0`; upstream `State::finalize` (`FillBase.cpp:2072`) erases
`points[polyline_end..]` UNCONDITIONALLY — an arch that stays inside the band
the whole trace (`polyline_end == 0`) discards everything. ares emitted the
full inner-boundary edge (±8.6mm lines) as "excess" arches; oracle emits none.
Fix: always `truncate(polyline_end)`. raft_layers/seeded excess edge lines gone;
remaining diff = interface-layer 1-unit E/coord rounding + M73 race.
`band_trace_keeps_boundary_run` pinned the buggy behavior — updated to assert
empty output matching upstream semantics.

### cviii. raft interface residual — f32 angle quantization applied; π/2 trig-arg offset remains
Upstream stores `base_angle`/`interface_angle`/`raft_angle_*` as `float` and
computes `cos(float_angle)` (f64 of the f32 angle). Port now round-trips all
raft angles through f32 (`fill_params.rs`, `fills.rs` rotate/rotate_back/
reference). This trimmed some endpoint diffs (3689→3609) but the interface
zigzag endpoints still differ by 1 unit (`X111.632` vs `X111.633` etc.):
ares's pipeline rotates contours by `-(angle + π/2)` and rotates back by
`+(angle + π/2)` (its vertical-line convention), while upstream uses
`cos(±angle)` directly — `cos(θ+π/2)` vs `-sin(θ)` differ in the last f64 bit,
so rotated-back endpoints land one lattice unit apart at rounding boundaries.
Full bit-parity needs the π/2 convention offset removed (use upstream's exact
`cos(angle)`/`sin(angle)` args end-to-end) — a deeper fill-pipeline refactor.
`raft_layers/min` residual after the edge-line fix is the narrow-region oracle
race (`;WIDTH:0.405676` sliver + F1982/1977), not a real defect.

### cix. Broad FAIL re-verification — races dominate, raft edge fix landed
Fresh re-slice of FAIL domains with the excess-arch fix + f32 angles:
- `bottom_surface_pattern/monotonic`, `top_surface_pattern/monotonic`,
  `gcode_comments/false`, `seam_slope_type/none`, `cool_plate_temp/min`,
  `sparse_infill_pattern/rectilinear`, `top_surface_pattern/archimedeanchords`
  → **byte-identical** (stale fixtures in the summary).
- `support_base_pattern/default`, `gcode_flavor/marlin2`,
  `activate_air_filtration_during_print`, `raft_layers/min` → diff signature is
  `WIDTH:0.405676/0.405678` medial slivers + `F1977/1982/2002/2005` cooling +
  `M73` — the CONFIRMED oracle TBB/hash races (narrow-region ordering + M73).
- `raft_layers/seeded` → excess edge lines FIXED (emitted as n=2 chains, now
  gone); residual = interface zigzag 1-unit endpoint rounding (π/2 trig-arg
  convention) + M73.
Remaining actionable: (a) remove the π/2 trig-arg convention offset for
bit-exact interface endpoints; (b) gcode_comments `move to first` on lifted
travels; (c) the raft `top_contacts`/`columns_base` upstream slice; (d) zigzag
`traverse` start residual. Everything else in the sweep tail is oracle-race
noise or stale fixtures.

### cx. mm3_per_mm f32 experiment — REVERTED
Tried casting `mm3_per_mm` operands through f32 (`Flow.cpp:219-228` computes
`float(m_height*(m_width - m_height*(1-0.25*PI)))`). REVERTED: it broke
`generates_absolute_e`/`first_layer_flow` tests (`0.0100000005` vs `0.01`) and
did NOT fix the `E.04556/57` perimeter diff — because ares's `width` input is
not the f32-truncated `m_width` upstream stores; casting a nearby f64 to f32 at
the accessor gives a wrong f32. Correct fix would propagate the f32 `Flow`
width from construction (`Flow::new_from_config_width` / `with_spacing`),
not truncate at `mm3_per_mm`. Deferred — sub-µm E residual.

### cxi. mm3_per_mm already f32-correct — E.04556 is ordering, not math
`perimeters/flow.rs::ordinary_volume` + `Flow::with_width` already compute
`mm3_per_mm` as `float(h*(w - h*(1-0.25*PI)))` (f32 operands, f64 expr, f32
result — exact `Flow.cpp:219-228` lattice). `path.mm3_per_mm` is therefore
already the f32 value feeding `for_length`. The accessor
`extrusion_per_mm_for_layer_with_width` (toolpath path) is a SEPARATE
pipeline; its f64 mm3 does not feed the emitted G-code `E`. The `E.04556/57`
perimeter residual is therefore NOT a flow-precision bug — on the seeded
`raft_layers` case it survives both formulations, confirming it is a
connectJunctions ordering artifact (oracle hash race) on a width-changing
segment, not flow math. No code change needed; revert kept.

### cxii. raft_layers seeded-case residual decomposed (oracle race + real gap)
`case-59tGO2` (raft_layers>1): oracle self-diff across two identical runs =
**223 lines** (confirmed TBB race floor). ares vs oracle ≈ 3609 — beyond the
race floor, so a real residual remains. Decomposition:
- Raft INTERFACE zigzag (lines ~295-362): 63 segments, **0 endpoint diffs** —
  `emit_loops_in_band` fix + f32 angles landed; geometry correct.
- ~600 extrusions differ (X∈[105,120] support region, spread across layers):
  same endpoints but different segmentation/E per line — `connect_base_support`
  zigzag produces equal endpoints with different internal segment splitting →
  per-line E differs though total is conserved. Plus M73 ordering race and
  `E.04556/57`/`E-.37619/20`/`E-.5661/9` sub-µm chamfer/wipe E.
- Conclusion: residual = `connect_base_support` zigzag SEGMENTATION parity
  (how a zigzag between boundary points is subdivided/emitted) — a real
  milestone-sized port gap, not a race and not flow math.

### cxiii. zigzag residual root-caused to graph cp ordering
The ~600-diff `connect_base_support` segmentation is NOT in `take_next`/
`append_limited`/`take_full_arc` (all faithful per-vertex emitters). It is
the ORDER `link_zigzag` iterates `map_infill_end_point_to_boundary` — which
pairs are connected depends on cp index order in the working graph. If
`build_working_graph` orders boundary intersections differently than
upstream, `take_next` pairs different infill lines → same endpoints but a
different chain topology → different per-segment E. NEXT MILESTONE: match
upstream's boundary-intersection ordering (the order intersections are
recorded per contour in `FillRectilinear`/`graph build`).

### cxiv. zigzag residual narrowed to connect_t_joints sort tie-break
`link_zigzag`/`close_left_caps`/`take_next`/`append_limited` all verified
structurally faithful (iteration = endpoint_index order, `index^1` other-end,
per-vertex boundary emit). The remaining ~600-line divergence localizes to
`connect_t_joints` (`FillBase.cpp:2625-2645`): upstream `std::sort` on
`SupportArcCost*` by `cost` DESCENDING is UNSTABLE — equal-cost candidates
reorder by libstdc++ introsort pointer movement. ares `sort_by` is STABLE,
preserving index order on ties. Equal-cost T-joint ties therefore resolve in
different order → different chains → same endpoints, different segmentation.
FIX: emulate upstream sort order (either make cost strictly ordered by adding
index tie-break matching upstream's pointer ordering, or reproduce the
introsort permutation — needs a probe of upstream's actual sort output order).

### cxv. residual lives in OBJECT sparse-infill connect, not raft support
`case-59tGO2` ~600-diff extrusions sit at lines 3537+ (object sparse infill,
~layer 40), NOT the raft (lines <362). Same XY endpoints, different per-line
E — e.g. `X116.19 Y102.518` alternating `E.07051`/`E.0293` — meaning ares
splits a zigzag run into different sub-segments than upstream (same total,
different per-line E). `ARES_CONN_PHASES` shows raft support n=0 (tiny region)
— the divergence is in the OBJECT's sparse-infill `connect_base_support`
zigzag/T-joint chain topology. Needs an instrumented upstream build
(INFILL_DEBUG_OUTPUT SVG or a dump probe of `take_next` pairing) to compare
chain topology directly; the stable-vs-unstable `std::sort` hypothesis is one
candidate but unproven. This is the next concrete milestone.

### cxvi. connect chain divergence — could_take gate ruled out
Probed: `vertical_dir` lacked upstream's `could_take_*` (`not_taken >
SCALED_EPSILON`) gate — added it, **no change** (3609 diff): the vertical
arches all satisfy the gate in this case. Reverted (no-effect change). Chain
histogram confirmed: oracle `n=86`/`n=79` vs ares `n=88` — ares connects one
extra infill line into the n=86 chain AND misses upstream's n=79 chain.
Upstream probe `/tmp/connprobe-bin` (ORCA_DUMP_CONN) + ares ARES_CONN_DUMP now
dump per-chain topology. NEXT: per-`take_next` decision trace — dump
(cp1,cp2,consumed,merged) at each take on both sides, find first divergent
pairing. Likely in select_support_arches cost ordering or connect_t_joints
unstable-sort order.

### cxvii. connect trace — call-count parity, chain inventory diverges
Built paired traces: oracle `connprobe`/`takeprobe` (ORCA_DUMP_CONN/
ORCA_TAKE_TRACE — global `cn` counter, NOT per-call reset) + ares
ARES_CONN_DUMP/ARES_TAKE_TRACE. Findings:
- `union_ex` returns **1 expolygon per raft layer** (expolygons=1 in=1) —
  no island-splitting. Connect invoked once per fill.
- ares chain inventory `n=64`(76)+`n=86`(80); oracle `n=64`+`n=86`+`n=88`+`n=79`
  — ares produces n=86 where oracle has n=86 AND n=79/88 variants. Same
  endpoints, different internal vertex count → chain pairing diverges.
- `could_take_*` gate on `vertical_dir` added+reverted (no effect). Base-vs-
  interface `ipSupportBase`/`ipRectilinear` pattern gating made diff WORSE
  (reverted — raft layers route to connect regardless of the pattern field
  in this port).
- Residual = connect_base_support chain PAIRING (which cp connects to which)
  — needs aligned per-take_next cp-index trace diff; `TAKE cp1=`/`cp2=`
  sequences differ in structure between ares (processes many cps/call) and
  oracle (repeats low cp indices per call), suggesting ares groups more
  infill ends into a single connect call's boundary set.

### cxviii. residual = missing contact-layer connect calls (spacing 0.407)
CIN/CONN probes: oracle `connect_base_support` inputs include
`infill=3/7 spacing=0.407080` calls (×38) that ares NEVER emits — ares uses
spacing=0.337 (`support_material_flow.spacing()` = `spacing(support_line_width
0.38, h 0.2)`) for all interface+contact layers. `0.407` = `spacing(0.45,0.2)`
= `line_width` (0.45) — the CONTACT/top-contact layer's `raft_interface_flow`
resolves to `line_width` (not `support_line_width`) for this config, OR a
separate `interface` flow path. ares's `interface_width = support_width`
(`bridge.rs:272`) collapses the distinction → contact layer gets 0.337 not
0.407, so those ~38 contact fills produce wrong spacing/lines → the ~600-line
E/segment divergence. FIX: `raft_interface_flow`/`contact` flow must resolve
its own width chain (`support_line_width>0 ? support_line_width : line_width`
per `Flow.cpp:262-267` — but the 0.407 implies `line_width` path); verify the
exact width source upstream then wire `interface_flow_spacing`/`contact
spacing` into `raft_layer_plans` for `RaftLayerKind::Contact`.

### cxix. contact spacing = line_width-derived (0.407), ares uses support (0.337)
ORCA_FLOW probe: `sm/smi/raft_i=0.337080, first=0.377080` — the FLOWS are all
0.337. Yet `connect_base_support` gets `spacing=0.407` for the `infill=3`
calls (raft interface) — so `filler->spacing` for those calls is NOT any flow
spacing; it is `spacing(line_width=0.45, h=0.2)=0.407` = the GENERIC
`line_width`. The raft interface filler spacing resolves to `line_width`
(0.45) not `support_line_width` (0.38) — meaning the connect `spacing` is the
fill `spacing` = `line_width`-based, OR the interface filler gets
`line_width` spacing via a path I have not pinned. ares computes interface
spacing from `support_line_width` → 0.337, producing wrong line_spacing/trim
→ ~600-line connect divergence. NEXT: pin the exact `filler->spacing` for the
raft interface filler in `fill_expolygons_with_sheath_generate_paths` — it is
`line_width`-derived (0.45→0.407), not support-derived.

### cxx. spacing 0.407 is this->spacing (=filler->spacing) — flow spacing itself
`connect_base_support(..., this->spacing)` — `this->spacing` is `Fill::spacing`
=`filler->spacing`, set to `support_material_interface_flow.spacing()` for
interface (`:1722`) / `raft_interface_flow.spacing()` for contact. ORCA_FLOW
showed `smi/raft_i=0.337`. So `filler->spacing` should be 0.337 — but the
connect dump shows 0.407. Either (a) `support_material_interface_flow.width()`
resolves to `line_width` (0.45) because `support_line_width` "0.38" is a
`coFloatOrPercent` whose `>0`/abs resolution differs from ares's, OR (b) the
filler spacing is overwritten between `:1722` and the connect call. NEXT:
probe `filler->spacing` (not `flow.spacing()`) right before
`fill_expolygons_with_sheath_generate_paths` — the Flow-vs-Fill `spacing`
distinction is the gap. ares uses `support_line_width`-derived spacing.

### cxxi. spacing resolution — connect gets this->spacing = filler->spacing
ORCA_FS probe (this->spacing at FillSupportBase::fill_surface) shows
`0.337080`/`0.377080` — the RAFT interface connect spacing IS 0.337 (matches
ares). So the `infill=3 spacing=0.407` connect_base_support calls are NOT raft
interface — `connect_base_support` is called ONLY at FillRectilinear.cpp:3623
(FillSupportBase). The 0.407 calls with infill=3 are therefore a DIFFERENT
connect_base_support caller OR FillSupportBase on a different surface
(object-level support infill). ares emits NO such calls → the ~600 diff is
MISSING connect calls (ares doesn't route some ipSupportBase fill through
connect_base_support), not a spacing bug. NEXT: which surfaces route to
FillSupportBase vs connect_infill — ares's sparse/support routing is missing
the ipSupportBase→connect_base_support path for the object infill region.

### cxxii. FINAL localization — interface connect spacing is line_width (0.45)
ORCA_FS: `FillSupportBase::fill_surface` gets `this->spacing=0.337/0.377`
(flange+base). But `connect_base_support` receives `spacing=0.407080` for the
`infill=3` calls — these are the raft INTERFACE layers (`ipSupportBase`
filler). `spacing(0.45,0.2)=0.40708` = `line_width` (0.45) — so the interface
filler's `spacing` resolves to `line_width`, NOT `support_line_width` (0.38→
0.337). Upstream `support_material_interface_flow` width =
`support_line_width>0 ? support_line_width : line_width`; the 0.407 value
implies the interface filler spacing uses `line_width` — i.e. ares resolves
`support_line_width` for interface where upstream effectively uses
`line_width` (likely a `FloatOrPercent` resolution or the `raft_interface`
flow uses the base `line_width`, not `support_line_width`). ares passes
`interface_width = support_width` → 0.337; needs `line_width` (0.45) → 0.407
for the CONNECT spacing specifically (the extruded `;WIDTH`/flow stays
interface-flow). This is a single width-source fix: `raft_layer_plans`
interface `spec.spacing` should be `spacing(line_width, height)`.

### cxxiii. The 0.407 calls are 39 MISSING FillSupportBase connects (infill=3)
ORCA_CIN: `connect_base_support` receives 39 calls with
`infill=3 boundary=1 spacing=0.407080` — `spacing(0.45,0.2)=0.407` =
`line_width`. ares emits ZERO connect_base_support calls with 0.407 — the

doesn't generate. ares's `cross_hatch` uses `connect_infill` (correct for
crosshatch). NEXT: probe WHICH surface/layer the 0.407 FillSupportBase calls
run on (z or surface id) to identify the unported `ipSupportBase` consumer.

### cxxiv. The 0.407 calls are the LOWEST-z (raft base) connects
`CIN` order: 39 `infill=3 boundary=1 spacing=0.407080` calls FIRST, then the
raft interface `infill=39/27 spacing=0.337080` calls. So the 0.407 calls are
the raft BASE layer (lowest z) — `filler_support`/`first_layer_flow`. But
`spacing(0.45,0.2)=0.407` = `line_width`, while `first_layer_flow` =

the raft base filler->spacing resolves to `line_width`, not
`initial_layer_line_width`/`support_line_width`. `infill=3` per call = the
raft base boundary splits into many small contours (39 blobs each ~3 lines).
NEXT: `first_layer_flow`/`filler_support` spacing for raft layer-0 should be
`line_width`-derived (0.45), not `initial_layer_line_width` (0.42→0.377) —
the 1st-layer support flow width chain resolves differently than ares's
`first_layer_width` (which uses `initial_layer_line_width`).

### cxxv. spacing=0.407 = line_width — but spec.spacing conflation
The raft BASE connect calls get `spacing=0.407080` = `line_width`(0.45)
(`spacing(0.45,0.2)`). ares uses `support_flow_spacing_mm`=0.337 for the base
`spec.spacing`. Setting base `spec.spacing`=`connect_spacing`=0.407 made the
diff WORSE (8227 vs 3609) because `spec.spacing` feeds `inner_offset`,
`line_spacing`(=`spacing/density`), and `x_margin` — all shift the zigzag
geometry, not just the connect. Upstream `FillSupportBase::fill_surface`
computes `line_spacing = scale_(this->spacing)/density` AND passes
`this->spacing` to `connect_base_support` — so `this->spacing`=0.407 for base
would change `line_spacing` too. Since oracle's base geometry matched ares's
0.337-spacing better, upstream's `line_spacing` uses 0.337 while the connect
`spacing` arg is 0.407 — meaning `FillSupportBase` splits `line_spacing` from
`this->spacing`, OR the connect `spacing` is `filler->spacing` post-modified.
NEXT: dump `line_spacing` (the zigzag pitch) in oracle `FillSupportBase` for
the base layer vs the `spacing` passed to connect — if they differ, ares's
`spec.spacing` must split into `line_spacing` (0.337) and `connect_spacing`
(0.407).

### cxxvi. spacing=0.407 pinned to a line_width FillSupportBase filler
ORCA_FLOW: `first_layer_flow.spacing()=0.377`, `support_material/interface/
raft_interface=0.337` — none is 0.407. `spacing(0.45,0.2)=0.407` = `line_width`

`filler_support`/`filler_raft_contact`/`filler_interface` spacings are all
0.337/0.377 — so the 0.407 calls are a `filler_support` on the raft base where
`support_material_flow`/`first_layer_flow` resolved to `line_width` (0.45)
instead of `support_line_width`/`initial_layer_line_width`. ares uses 0.337 for
these blob regions → ~600-line divergence. NEXT: `support_material_flow` for
the raft base resolves `support_line_width` — if upstream's `support_line_width`

ORCA_FSUP: raft base `filler_support->spacing = support_material_flow` = 0.337

ORCA_FBS probe inside `FillSupportBase::fill_surface` (the ONLY

flight (~10min). Expected: `flow.width`≈0.196/`spacing`≈0.1536/`mm3`≈0.0307 —
confirming the skirt `width` resolves to a non-`initial_layer_line_width`
value (or `height`/`spacing` differs). This closes the skirt `mm3_per_mm`
residual's identity question.

### cliv. ORCA_SF probe — captures skirt_flow Flow directly (fixed recursion)
Fixed the probe: `Flow _sf = Flow::new_from_config_width(...)` captures the
return value, then `fprintf` dumps `_sf.width/spacing()/height/mm3_per_mm()`
and `return _sf`. Build in flight. This resolves whether the oracle skirt
`flow.width` is ~0.196 (a non-`initial_layer_line_width` source) or the

`connect_base_support` calls at `infill=3` but not their `filler`/`this`
identity — this skirt probe is the next targeted identity check.

### clv. ORCA_SF probe rebuild — width()/height() are methods not fields
The `Flow` struct has `width()`/`height()`/`spacing()`/`mm3_per_mm()` as METHODS
(`Flow.hpp:61-65`), not fields. First probe build failed on `.width`/`.height`
field access. Fixed to `_sf.width()`/`_sf.height()`/`_sf.spacing()`/
`_sf.mm3_per_mm()` — rebuild in flight (~10min). When built: `ORCA_SF=1` slice
dumps the actual skirt `flow.width`/`spacing`/`height`/`mm3_per_mm` —
confirming `width≈0.196`/`spacing≈0.1536` (the `line_width`-fallback source)
or a `height`/`spacing` divergence.

### clvi. ORCA_SF: skirt_flow mm3_per_mm=0.0754 — E is scaled by spacing(line_width)
`ORCA_SF` probe: `skirt_flow w=0.42 sp=0.377 h=0.2 mm3=0.0754` — ares's
`mm3_per_mm`=0.0754 is CORRECT (matches `skirt_flow`). But oracle `E.04556` for
`1.4835mm` → effective `mm3`=0.0307 = `0.0754·0.407` = `skirt_mm3·spacing
(line_width=0.45,0.2)`. The emitted `E` = `mm3_per_mm·length·0.407` — the
extrusion is scaled by `spacing(line_width)` — a `line_width`-spacing ratio
applied to the skirt `E`. `0.407`=`spacing(0.45,0.2)`. So the skirt extrusion
`E` = `skirt_flow.mm3_per_mm·length·spacing(line_width)` — the `line_width`
spacing acts as an extrusion multiplier. This is NOT a `flow.width`/`height`
bug — it's an `E`-emission scaling by `spacing(line_width)`. RESIDUAL: skirt
`E` = `mm3_per_mm·length·spacing(line_width)` — a `line_width`-spacing
extrusion multiplier ares doesn't apply (ares emits `mm3_per_mm·length`
directly). Likely a `flow_ratio`/`extrusion_ratio` applied in the oracle's
skirt `E` emit that ares misses.

   (0.407), height:mm3_per_mm(first_layer)(0.0754)}` — `spacing`/`height`
   crossed on the emit path (not `skirt_flow`'s spacing=0.377/height=0.2).
Both need emit-path `flow`/`spacing`/`filler` IDENTITY probes — which `Flow`/
`filler` object the `G1 E`/`connect_base_support` emit uses. Baseline 3609
held through all reverts; tests green; committed through `2851e72b`. The
residuals are isolated to the `line_width`-substitution on emit paths; the
geometry/`spacing`/`mm3_per_mm` triad needs separate `width` sources per
upstream's `filler->spacing` vs `flow` split.

### clxxvii. Skirt E mm3_per_mm = spacing(line_width)·mm3_per_mm(first_layer) — CONFIRMED
`mm3_per_mm = spacing(0.45,0.2)·mm3_per_mm(0.42,0.2)` = `0.407·0.0754` = `0.0307`.
Predicted `E` for 1.4835mm = `0.0455` vs oracle `0.0456` — 0.0004 error.
`flow` for the skirt `E` = `{width:first_layer_width(0.42), spacing:
spacing(line_width)=0.407, height:mm3_per_mm(first_layer)=0.0754}` —
`mm3_per_mm = spacing·height` = `spacing(line_width)·mm3_per_mm(first_layer)`.
The `height` field holds `mm3_per_mm(first_layer)` (0.0754), `spacing` holds
`spacing(line_width)` (0.407), `width` holds `first_layer_width` (0.42). This
is a `flow` where `spacing`=`line_width`-spacing, `height`=`first_layer`-mm3,
`width`=`first_layer`-width — `spacing`/`height`/`width` all crossed vs
`skirt_flow`(spacing=0.377, height=0.2, width=0.42). RESIDUAL: the `G1 E` emit
`flow` for the skirt uses `spacing`=`spacing(line_width)`, `height`=

crossing with `line_width` substituted for `first_layer` in `spacing`.

`flow` construction assigns `spacing`=`spacing(line_width)` and `height`=
`mm3_per_mm(first_layer)` — a `spacing`/`height`/`mm3` field confusion.
RESIDUAL: locate the emit-path `flow` construction — `Flow` with

### clxxx. Full-sweep mass triage — divergence class histogram (2026-09-23)
Whole `${TMP_ROOT}/ares-parity-fresh` sweep re-measured: 2021 cases with both
`ares.gcode`+`orca.gcode`. Diff-line histogram (vs oracle, `generated by`
filtered, diff-count ≤2 = pass):
- **pass(≤2): 1244 (61.5%)**
- 3–50: 93
- 51–200: 629  ← the dominant failing band
- 201–1000: 49
- \>1000: 6 (all raft cases; `case-59tGO2`=5016, `case-wcOWKK`=4993,
  `case-n4acJn`=4917, `case-86PKVP`=4881 — the raft line_width-fallback
  residuals of §clxxix plus raft structural slices)

Diff-line class histogram across a 120-case random sample (6027 diff lines):
- **M73 ordering 1659 (27.5%)** — confirmed oracle TBB/hash race, not fixable
  byte-for-byte (lines present both sides, position shifted).
- **G1-other ordering 1641 (27%)** — same-line reordering pairs (`135a136`,
  `443d442/444a444`) — emit sequencing, partly oracle race, partly real.
- **bare-F cooling feedrate 1595 (26%)** — `F2005→F2001`, `F1982→F1973`,
  `F1982→F1978`: ares consistently ~0.2% HIGHER (less slowdown). NOT a fixed
  delta → the slowdown math inputs (`line.time`/`total_time`/`adjustable`
  tagging) drift, not a constant offset. `slowdown.rs`/`parse.rs` are already
  faithful ULP-annotated `CoolingBuffer.cpp` ports; the asymmetry lives in the
  adjustable-tagging / measured-time front-end, needs an oracle SD-dump probe.
- travelF9k 453, comments 353 (missing `;WIDTH:` / `;LAYER` emits),
  pure-retract 326 (wipe-retract split `E-3.79861 F2399` vs `E-2.8 F1800`).

CONCLUSION: no single dominant fixable constant. The 51–200 band mass is
oracle-ordering noise (M73+G1 reorder ≈55%) layered over a real cooling-
feedrate ULP drift (~26%) and smaller retract/comment residuals. The feedrate
and ordering classes are the highest-leverage remaining targets but both are
probe-cycle-bound (10–25 min oracle builds), same cost class as §clxxix.

### clxxxi. bare-F cooling feedrate LOCALIZED to an extra _EXTRUDE_SET_SPEED block
ORCA_CBDUMP probe (`/tmp/cbdump.nix` → `/tmp/cbdump-bin`, instrumented
`CoolingBuffer::calculate_layer_slowdown` after `sort_lines_by_decreasing_
feedrate` at `CoolingBuffer.cpp:657`, dumps per-line `type/len/feedrate/time/
time_max/adjustable` + `n_lines_adjustable`/`total`/`nonadj` per layer).

On `case-03RaeG` the slowdown INPUTS are **byte-identical** to ares on the
matching layers (`total=2.85214233`, `nadj=3`, `nonadj=0.294752866`, per-line
`len/feedrate/time/time_max` equal to 6+ digits). The F2005-vs-F2001 /
F1982-vs-F1973 divergence is NOT a slowdown-math ULP — it is a **line-count
difference in the adjustable set**: ares emits an EXTRA `_EXTRUDE_SET_SPEED`
block on 4 layers (oracle nadj=7/nlines=41, ares nadj=8/nlines=44 on the
`total=8.32` layer). The extra ares line is a `len=0.2236 fr=50` adjustable
extrusion oracle folds into a neighbouring same-speed block; its `t=0.004472`
inflates `total` (8.336950 vs 8.3244524, Δ≈0.0125) and shifts
`new_feedrate_to_reach_time_stretch` → ares keeps more lines at the higher
feedrate (F2005/F1982) while oracle drops to F2001/F1973.

ROOT: emit-side `_EXTRUDE_SET_SPEED` block grouping — ares opens one more
block than oracle for a `len≈0.22` segment (likely gap-fill/short-infill where
oracle reuses the prior same-speed group). NOT in `slowdown.rs`/`parse.rs`
(both faithful ports, verified against `CoolingBuffer.cpp:46-300`); the bug is
in the extrusion-emit grouping that decides when to open a fresh SET_SPEED
block (`motion/extrusion.rs::speed`, `motion/path.rs`). Next probe: dump
oracle's `;_EXTRUDE_SET_SPEED` block open/close decisions (`GCode.cpp`
`extrude_path`/`_add_speed_change`) to find the merge condition ares misses.

### clxxxii. bare-F root cause CONFIRMED = upstream infill path-grouping, not cooling
Follow-up on §clxxxi: the extra `len=0.2236 fr=50` `_EXTRUDE_SET_SPEED` block
is ares emitting a **two-segment corner micro-fragment**
(`X106.024 Y113.928 E.00264` + `X106.1 Y113.963 E.00265`, `;WIDTH:0.429544`)
preceded by a `F9000` reposition — a narrow-region/gap-fill fragment oracle
does NOT emit as a separate path. Oracle covers the same X106/Y114 region with
a contiguous crosshatch/grid infill (alternating full-diagonal lines). So the
extra cooling line is a SYMPTOM of an upstream **infill/narrow-region path-
grouping divergence** (the documented narrow-region `split_lines`
reconstruction residual, todo #6), which then shifts the slowdown Newton
feedrate. The cooling-buffer port (`slowdown.rs`,`parse.rs`) is verified
correct against oracle inputs — do NOT re-tune it. Real fix lives in the
infill path entity grouping (`fill/`/`prepare_infill`/`narrow`), same locus as
the narrow-region residual.

### clxxxiii. CRITICAL — true pass rate ~94%; stored-sweep orca.gcode is a stale snapshot
Re-verification methodology fix: the sweep's stored `orca.gcode` is a SINGLE
oracle run snapshot; OrcaSlicer is internally non-deterministic (TBB order +
`ankerl::unordered_dense` hash-order). Comparing ares to a stale snapshot
overcounts failures. Correct metric: run oracle TWICE fresh; a case passes iff
ares matches either run within oracle's own self-variance
(`diff(ares,oracle_run) <= max(2, oracle_self_diff)`).

Measured:
- `case-03RaeG`: oracle-self=104..158, ares=93..107 → ares is CLOSER to each
  oracle run than the two runs are to each other. AT NOISE FLOOR.
- `case-090y6B`, `case-1Zs34c`: oracle-self=0, ares=0 → byte-identical (were
  "failing" only vs the stale snapshot).
- 20-case re-verify of "failing" band: **16/20 byte-identical** to fresh
  oracle; the other 4 (`RZBkgs/79uhKJ/5uE5fL/7dVM9D`) ares-vs-B=0 — all 20
  actually pass. Mid-band mass is ~100% oracle noise.
- 80-case random sample, oracle×2 + ares: **pass=75 (93.75%), real_fail=5**,
  oracle_fail=0. Real failures: `n4acJn` (raft, 4818, deterministic), and
  `W5UpLS/N4qtIk/vlWuXt/jS9NwP` (ares 57-236 vs self 51-152 — small real
  divergence on noisy oracle inputs).

CONCLUSION: true parity is ~94%, not 61.5%. The remaining REAL deterministic
residual is the raft support-layer structural slice (`n4acJn`-family, the 6
>1000-diff cases). The §clxxxi-clxxxii cooling/narrow-fragment analysis still
holds as a real (but small) divergence; it is dwarfed by oracle noise on most
cases. Highest-leverage remaining work = the raft structural residual and a
fresh dual-oracle sweep to get the true per-case pass map.

### clxxxiv. raft residual = base-polygon footprint ~0.42mm oversized (deterministic)
`case-n4acJn` (raft_layers=100, raft_expansion=1.5, raft_first_layer_expansion
=2, line_width=0.45, first_layer_line_width=0.42, support_line_width=0.38,
skirt_distance=2, skirt_loops=2) is a DETERMINISTIC divergence (oracle self=0,
ares=4818). The earliest geometry diff is the `;TYPE:Skirt` rounded-rect
corner — but the root is the raft base polygon feeding the hull:
- oracle raft base sheath outline spans X `101.059–118.941` (≈17.88mm),
- ares raft base sheath outline spans X `100.639–119.361` (≈18.72mm),
- Δ ≈ 0.42mm per side = exactly `first_layer_line_width` (0.42).
The sheath shrink `0.5·flow.scaled_width()` (`SupportCommon.cpp:738`) is
faithful in `raft/fills.rs:73`; the oversized term is in the BASE polygon
expansion (`raft/polygons.rs:73-93` — `raft_expansion`/`first_layer_expansion`
/`expansion_to_slice` chain). Ares expands the first-layer raft polygon ~0.42mm
too far. Downstream this shifts the skirt convex-hull corner (oracle corner
arcs at 98.494/101.059/118.941 vs ares 98.472/100.639/119.361) and every
support/object gcode region. NEXT: probe `generate_raft_base`/raft-layer-0
polygon expansion (`SupportCommon.cpp:279-281,366-371`) to find the extra
~0.42mm term — likely `first_layer_expansion` applied on top of an
already-expanded base, or a `0.5·width`/`spacing` boundary term ares double-adds.

### clxxxv. raft flange sheath emitted at +0.5·width (GROW) not −0.5·width (SHRINK)
`case-n4acJn` deep trace (ARES_INNER_CONTOUR dump + gcode inspection):
- ares `raft_polygons.first_layer` octagon = ±9.15mm flat edges (18.30mm).
- `raft_layer_polylines` computes `closing = offset2_ex(first_layer, +ε,
  −(ε+0.5·width))` = **8.94mm radius — CORRECT**, matching oracle sheath
  (`X101.059–118.941`). The sheath polyline out[0] = closing contour = 8.94.
- BUT the emitted `;TYPE:Support` (Z:0.2 flange) sheath outline = a ROUNDED
  RECT at radius **9.361** (= `first_layer 9.15 + 0.5·width` = `closing 8.94 +
  width 0.42`), with corner arcs — i.e. `first_layer` DILATED by +0.5·width
  with round joins, NOT the `closing` eroded contour.
- The SECOND `;TYPE:Support` block (base raft layers, `WIDTH:0.38`) is
  byte-identical to oracle — only the flange/layer-0 sheath diverges.
ROOT: the emitted flange sheath is drawn on `first_layer` offset +0.5·width
(round), not on `closing`. `materialize_layer`/`raft_layer_polylines` produces
the correct 8.94 sheath, so either (a) the flange emit path draws a separate
`first_layer`-boundary perimeter, or (b) `plan.polygons` reaching emit differs
from the `raft_polygons.first_layer` that fed the dump. NEXT: instrument the
emitted sheath polyline at the `materialize_layer`→`FillExtrusionPath`→motion
boundary to confirm whether `out[0]` (8.94) reaches the writer or is replaced.

### clxxxvi. BOTH sweep sides stale — current binary fixes raft flange sheath
**The stored `ares.gcode` is as stale as `orca.gcode`.** Re-slicing
`case-n4acJn` with the CURRENT ares binary: `diff(current, stored_ares)=2362`.
The flange sheath that appeared "emitted at +0.5·width" in §clxxxv is now
BYTE-IDENTICAL to fresh oracle (`X118.941 Y117.892 E.485`) — the raft
flange-sheath residual was an old-build artifact, already fixed in-tree.

Current-binary vs fresh-oracle on `n4acJn`: total diff 3182 (was 4818 stale),
non-M73 2929. Breakdown: ~614 ares lines share XY with an oracle line
(E-rounding `E.04556`/`E.04557` last-digit + reordering), ~994 lines have no
matching oracle XY (real geometry). The REAL residual on raft cases is now
E-rounding last-digit + ordering + a smaller geometry delta — NOT the flange
footprint.

METHODOLOGY CORRECTED: any parity measurement MUST regenerate BOTH sides with
current binaries (fresh oracle + fresh ares), because OrcaSlicer is
non-deterministic AND the ares output changes as fixes land. All prior sweep
diff counts (incl. the 61.5%/94% figures) are lower bounds distorted by
staleness on both sides. A full dual-fresh re-slice is required for the true
per-case pass map.

### clxxxvii. TRUE pass ~95% (57/60) dual-fresh; residuals are estimator/M73 + raft-59tGO2
Full dual-fresh re-verification (current ares binary + fresh oracle ×2) on a
60-case random sample: **TRUE PASS 57/60 = 95%**, oracle_fail=0, real_fail=3:
- `case-59tGO2` (self=0, ares=3609): the documented deterministic raft

- `case-ArkzGA` (self=149, ares 250/353): exceeds oracle self-variance — real

- `case-aBhv1I` (self=0, ares=142): **pure ordering** — every diff is a

CONCLUSION: the only large *deterministic* residual left is the `59tGO2` raft
family; the M73/estimator time-model drift produces the deterministic
ordering-only failures; everything else is oracle non-determinism. The sweep's
stored outputs are stale on BOTH sides and must be regenerated fresh to
measure true parity.

### clxxxviii. case-59tGO2 residual = wipe-E split + ordering (deterministic)
Re-verified on CURRENT binary + fresh deterministic oracle (self=0,
ares=3609). The diff is dominated by:
1. **wipe-E per-segment last-digit** — `E-.16478` vs `E-.16476`,
   `E-.70571`/`E-.70573`, `E-.37619`/`E-.3762`, `E.04556`/`E.04557`:
   identical XY, ±1–2 in the 5th E decimal, repeated uniformly across layers.
   Locus: `motion/travel.rs:230` `retraction = during·(segment_length /
   distribution_distance)`; `during = (retraction_feedrate/60 ·
   retraction_distance / wipe_speed).min(remaining)` (`:192`), where
   `retraction_distance`/`distribution_distance`/`wipe_speed`/`segment_length`
   each carry sub-ULP candidates (`wipe_path.rs` clip_end truncation,
   `scaled_distance` rounding, `role_based_wipe_speed` feedrate at wipe).
   A systematic ~1e-5 split difference — the documented wipe-E residual.
   Needs an oracle `Wipe::wipe` (`GCode.cpp:456-462`) per-segment
   `during`/`distribution_distance`/`dE` probe.
2. Reordering pairs (`67a68`, `160a161`) — M73/emit ordering + oracle hash
   variance.
NOTE: the parity TEST harness (`orca_parity.rs::compare_exported_for`) already
re-slices the oracle up to `ARES_ORCA_RUNS`=3 on divergence — the suite
correctly treats oracle non-determinism; only `/tmp` sweep artifacts were
stale-snapshot comparisons. Test methodology is sound.

### clxxxix. wipe-E residual narrowed to wipe-path length, NOT wipe_speed
`case-59tGO2` wipe-E `±1–2e-5` residual narrowed: emitted wipe `F` values match
oracle exactly (`F3000`/`F2100`/`F2400`) → `wipe_speed` is correct, so
`during = retract_fr/60·retraction_distance/wipe_speed` differs only via
`retraction_distance` (the clipped wipe-path length). Oracle `Wipe::wipe`
(`GCode.cpp:441-460`): `wipe_dist=scale_(wipe_distance)`, `clip_end(length−
wipe_dist)`, `dE=length·(segment_length/wipe_dist)`. Ares ports all of it
(`wipe_path.rs`,`travel.rs:230`). The residual is the clipped wipe-path LENGTH
(`total_length`/`retraction_distance`) differing by a sub-lattice-unit — the
`clip_end` truncation factor `clip/lsqr.sqrt()` (`Polyline.cpp:52-72`) or the
scaled-int point rounding feeding `scaled_distance`. Deterministic; needs an
oracle `Wipe::wipe` probe dumping `wipe_dist`/`length`/per-segment
`segment_length`+`dE` to localize which input drifts. (The `F3381/F2963`,
`F1982/F1913` diffs are the separate cooling-feedrate/narrow-fragment residual.)

### cxc. wipe-E residual: ares re-rounds stored path ints, oracle does not
Oracle `Wipe::wipe` (`GCode.cpp:344-360`): `wipe_path = last_pos +
path.points[1:]` — `path.points` are ALREADY `coord_t` scaled ints used RAW;
`wipe_path_length = min(wipe_path.length(), wipe_dist)`;
`retractionDuringWipe = retraction_speed·unscale_(wipe_path_length)/wipe_speed`.
Ares (`wipe_path.rs:40-43`) re-rounds each `wipe_path` point through
`(point.x/scale_factor).round() as i64` — if the stored mm floats are not
exactly `int·scale`, `.round()` snaps them to a different lattice point than
oracle's raw ints → `total_length`/`retraction_distance`/per-segment
`segment_length` shift → `during` and `dE` shift by ~1e-5 (the observed wipe-E
residual). HYPOTHESIS: drop the re-round of `wipe_path[1:]` (use the raw
scaled ints oracle uses) — but only if ares's `wipe_path` mm values are
guaranteed `int·scale` round-trips. ORCA_WIPEDUMP probe building
(`/tmp/wipeprobe.nix`, WIPECALL len/wipedist/wspeed + WSEG slen/dE/bx/by) to
compare oracle `wipe_dist`/`length`/`slen`/`dE` against ares's before changing.

### cxci. wipe-E residual isolated: clipped wipe-path endpoint ~3 scaled units
ORCA_WIPEDUMP (oracle) on case-59tGO2: `WIPEPRE len=1.2 wipedist=1000000`,
`WSEG slen=313497.206 dE=0.376196647` → oracle emits `E-.3762`; ares emits
`E-.37619`. `dE = 1.2·(slen/1e6)`; ares's `dE` lands below the `.376195`
rounding boundary → ares's `slen` ≈ 313494 vs oracle 313497 → **ares's clipped
wipe-path segment endpoint differs by ~3 scaled units (~3e-6 mm)**. Source:
the `clip_end` truncating endpoint (`wipe_path.rs` `clip/lsqr.sqrt()` factor +
`as i64`, vs `Polyline.cpp:52-72`) OR the `scaled_position`/`unscaled_position`
round-trip on the wipe-path points (`wipe_path.rs:40` re-round + `:113`
unscale). `during`/`length` = 1.2 and `wipe_dist` = 1e6 match exactly; only
the segment length carries the 3-unit drift. Next: dump ares's per-segment
`slen`/`retraction` to confirm which endpoint (clip-truncated last point or a
mid-path rounded point) is off.

### cxcii. wipe residual ROOT = wipe-path geometry, not just E-split
Dual dumps (oracle ORCA_WIPEDUMP vs ares ARES_WIPE_SEG) on case-59tGO2 show
the wipe-path POLYLINE POINTS differ ~1-11 scaled units (~1e-6–1e-5 mm), not
merely the dE distribution:
  oracle slen: 313497.206 471742 771322.687 228678 137316 274592.191 588092
  ares   slen: 313491.549 471748.000 771311.917 228689 137315 274590.776 588095
`during`=1.2 and `wipe_dist`=1e6 match exactly in both. The wipe path is the
tail of the just-emitted extrusion path (the loop being wiped); the stored
path tail points differ → different wipe segments → both the wipe XY
endpoints AND the per-segment dE differ. Root cause is upstream: which
points get stored as `wipe_path` (`path/constant.rs:95`, `variable.rs:67`,
`loop_paths.rs:78`) vs oracle `Wipe::path` (`this->path.points`) — the
extrusion path's scaled-int tail differs ~1 unit per point. This is a
path-geometry residual (the emitted-loop point selection/rounding), deeper
than E formatting. ARES_WIPE_SEG dump added at `travel.rs:229` for the diff.

### cxciii. wipe-path residual = stored extrusion-path tail point selection
The wipe-path `slen` deltas (~1-11 scaled units) trace to `state.wipe_path =
wipe_points.rev()` (`path/constant.rs:95`, `variable.rs:67`, `path.rs:247`,
`loop_paths.rs:78`) storing the emitted path's points — oracle `Wipe::path` =
`this->path.points` (the `ExtrusionPath`/`ThickPolyline` scaled ints). The
stored path tail differs from oracle's by ~1 int/point → the SOURCE extrusion
path (the perimeter loop being wiped) carries different scaled-int points.
This is the same upstream surface as the documented `narrow/split.rs`
path-grouping residual: the emitted extrusion-path POINT SELECTION diverges
from `PerimeterGenerator`/`ExtrusionEntityCollection` before the wipe emit
runs. Fixing requires the loop-path point list to match upstream at scaled-int
precision — a PerimeterGenerator-path parity issue, not a wipe-emit bug.
The `ARES_WIPE_SEG`/`ORCA_WIPEDUMP` dumps (both built and verified) give a
direct per-segment harness for re-verifying after any path-selection fix.

### cxciv. wipe-path source corrected to raw path.polyline; residual = perimeter int quantization
Option-coverage re-run (`raft_layers` domain): min PASS, max/seeded DIVERGENT
— but the divergence is the SAME wipe-path `dE` ±1e-5 + M73-ordering family as
case-59tGO2, NOT a raft_layers gate (the earlier summary's "unsupported" was
stale). Fixed the wipe-path SOURCE: `state.wipe_path` was built from
`local_points` AFTER `arc::clip_fitting_end` may `project_to_circle` the last
point (non-lattice mm); upstream stores the raw `path.polyline.points`
(`GCode.cpp:6111`). Now `wipe_points_source` = the pre-clip `local_points`
snapshot feeding all three wipe-path consumers (`path.rs` slope branch,
`variable::emit`, `constant::emit` whose `local_points` field was renamed
`wipe_points`). Verified no regression: case-090y6B still byte-identical,
case-03RaeG still matches an oracle run byte-exact. This did NOT fix
case-59tGO2 (clip_fitting_end doesn't fire on its paths) — the residual
there is the perimeter loop's scaled-int points differing ~1-11 units from
oracle's `path.polyline` (PerimeterGenerator/ExtrusionPath int quantization),
a deeper path-generation parity issue, not a wipe-emit bug. Correctness fix
kept: arc-fitted paths now wipe on the raw polyline like upstream.

### cxcv. option-coverage verdict: 645/649 PASS; wipe residual = perimeter int quantization
Live `ARES_PARITY_OPTIONS=1` option sweep result (`option-coverage-summary.md`):
645/649 compared domains PASS, 4 FAIL — `line_width` (legit upstream reject),
`raft_layers` (max/seeded → the wipe-path family below), `sparse_infill_pattern`
(adaptivecubic unported), `top_surface_pattern` (octagramspiral island-lifecycle
= same wipe family), `wall_generator` (arachne unported). ~99.4% option-domain
byte-parity. The wipe residual root is now confirmed PERIMETER-PATH int
quantization: `path.polyline` scaled-int points differ ~1-11 units on the
octagon/chamfer segments vs oracle (`slen` 313497 vs 313491 etc.), so both the
wipe segment lengths AND the emitted wipe-path XY tail differ. This is a
PerimeterGenerator/ExtrusionPath point-generation parity issue (the emitted
XY match at 3 decimals; the underlying coord_t ints differ), the same upstream
surface as narrow/split.rs. Deepest residual class; not a wipe-emit fix.

### cxcvi. wipe-path accumulation: oracle concatenates ALL paths, ares keeps last
Emitted perimeter XY is BYTE-IDENTICAL ares-vs-oracle (the loop coords match at
full precision) — so the wipe-path `slen` diff is NOT the path geometry. Root:
upstream `GCode.cpp:5980-5989` builds `m_wipe.path` by iterating `for path :
paths` over the WHOLE ExtrusionEntityCollection, appending each
`path.polyline.to_polyline()` (skipping the dup joint point when contiguous).
Ares instead sets `state.wipe_path = <last emitted path>.local_points` inside
`path::emit` — only the FINAL path's polyline, not the accumulated collection.
When the collection has >1 path (multi-segment loop), oracle's wipe path spans
the concatenation (different tail/length → different clip_end → the observed
~1-11-unit slen diffs). ORCA_WIPEPATH probe building to dump oracle's stored
`Wipe::path.points` for direct comparison. If confirmed, fix = accumulate
`wipe_path` across the collection's paths (dedup joint) rather than overwrite.

### cxcvii. wipe-path point COUNT matches; residual = sub-emitted-precision int coords
ORCA_WIPEPATH (oracle) vs ARES_DUMP_PATH (ares) on case-59tGO2: both store an
18-point octagon `Wipe::path` (n=18 both). So the accumulation/`for path:
paths` concatenation is NOT the divergence (same point count). The oracle
points are object-local coords (±11.1e6); ares's are global-plate coords —
different frames, so compare via emitted wipe XY which ARE byte-identical at
3 decimals. The `slen` diffs (313497.206 vs 313491.549 ≈ 5.7 scaled units ≈
5.7e-6 mm) are therefore a SUB-3-DECIMAL int-precision difference in the
perimeter path's `coord_t` points — the emitted XY round to the same 3
decimals but the underlying ints differ in the ~4th-6th mm decimal. This is a
PerimeterGenerator/offset int-quantization residual (the contour offset /
thick-polyline points land on slightly different lattice ints), the deepest
and last class of wipe residual — the wipe EMIT is now verified faithful
(during/dist/clip all correct); only the input polyline's int coords differ.

### cxcviii. wipe residual pinpointed: per-loop contour int quantization
Frame-independent segment-length compare (oracle WPATH vs ares ASEGP): the
FIRST wipe's segments match exactly (`821443`/`178557` both). Divergence
appears on LATER octagon loops (different radii): oracle `313497.206` vs ares
`313491.549`, oracle `471742` vs ares `471748`, etc. So specific perimeter
loops have `coord_t` points a few lattice units off from oracle — the contour
offset (`offset`/thick-polyline generation) produces ints differing in the
~4th-6th mm decimal. The emitted XY round to the same 3 decimals but the wipe
`dE` (computed from int `Line::length()`) flips the 5th decimal. This is a
PerimeterGenerator offset-precision residual — the SAME upstream surface as
the narrow/split and raft residuals (all reduce to Clipper1 offset / contour
point ints differing by a few units on specific geometries). The wipe emit
itself is verified byte-faithful. Fix requires offset-contour int parity —
a milestone-scale PerimeterGenerator investigation, not a wipe bug.

## §ccviii FillLockedZag + horiz_move port (e28b3504); multi-angle rectilinear family
- crosszag/lockedzag were silently dropped in fill_entities emit
  (`Configured(_) => {}`), not a geometry divergence — zero sparse output.

  CrossZag/LockedZag (Fill.cpp:1311-1316); applied as x0 grid shift +
  `gap_line` odd-column (FillRectilinear.cpp:2811-2816). Rust/C both
  truncate int division toward zero — `horiz_move` i64 semantics match.
- `fill::locked_zag::fill_surface_locked_zag` (FillRectilinear.cpp:3866):
  skin ring (inset skin_infill_depth) + skeleton, per-density
  fill_rectilinear_surface, then per-flow region intersect assigns
  (Polyline, Flow). Reuses group_fills lock_region_param.
- REMAINING unported generators all share `fill_surface_by_multilines` +
  `make_fill_lines` (FillRectilinear.cpp:2996/2920): triangles, stars,
  cubic, quartercubic, lateral-lattice, lateral-honeycomb. Each differs only
  in the (angle_base, pattern_shift) sweep list; the shared machinery needs
  ExPolygonWithOffset + slice_region_by_vertical_lines + connect_infill.
  Ares's fill_rectilinear is a single-direction equivalent that does NOT
  expose the raw-segment make_fill_lines stage — the multiline port needs
  that lower-level generator. tpmsd/tpmsfk/lightning are independent large
  generators.

## §ccix Multi-angle rectilinear + TPMS-D emit (e70d77f1/91c9edbd/0590226c)

  - TriHexagon=ipStars (PrintConfig.cpp:238): 3×60° + (3/2)·s·m/d shift.
  - QuarterCubic: 4 sweeps {0,±dx1},{π/2,±dx2}, z-dependent dx (3518).
  - LateralLattice: 2×90° sweeps tan(angle_{1,2})·z, reversed odd layers (3449).
  - LateralHoneycomb: inverted-Y single/double family by vertical-period
    phase (3550); uses region infill_overhang_angle + lateral_lattice params
    (already plumbed into SurfaceFillParams).
  - All override _layer_angle→0 so params.angle+π/2 == _infill_direction.
- anchor_projection whitelist += TriHexagon/QuarterCubic/LateralLattice/
  LateralHoneycomb/TpmsD (upstream anchors ALL patterns via fill_surface).
- FillTpmsD ported (fill/tpms_d.rs): analytic make_waves + -45° rotate +

## §ccxi FillTpmsFK + marchsq ported (12981710); Lightning is the sole remaining generator
- marchsq marching-squares ported (geometry/marching_squares.rs): bit-packed

## §ccxiii interpolate_edge binary search + InfillPattern STL subset clarified
- marching_squares::interpolate_edge switched from a linear edge walk to a
  lower_bound binary search over the edge's raster pixels (MarchingSquares.hpp
  lower_bound(e.from,e.to,v)) — O(log window) field evals per ring vertex
  instead of O(window); Coord::manhattan_along added for the edge length.
- Confirmed options::InfillPattern (STL path) is a 12-variant subset; the
  advanced patterns parse via ProcessInfillPattern on the 3MF/project path,
  which is what the sparse_infill_pattern sweep exercises. tpmsfk emits there.

## §ccxiv tpmsfk raster cost + FillLightning = the final structural port
- tpmsfk marchsq over the rsize=0.004mm raster is compute-heavy (field evals +
  dense ring polylines feeding connect_infill); interpolate_edge is now a
  lower_bound binary search and scan_rings has a degenerate-direction guard,
  but the sweep case still exceeds the per-slice budget — the pattern is ported
  and compiles; matching upstream's sub-second cost needs the ex_tbb-parallel
  structure + a perf pass (connect_infill on dense rings is the suspect).
- FillLightning is the SOLE remaining generator and is architecturally
  different: PrintObject::prepare_lightning_infill_data builds a per-object
  Lightning::Generator (PrintObject.cpp:1010) that walks ALL layers top-to-
  bottom, computes m_overhang_per_layer (internal-surface overhang subtraction),
  then generateTrees propagates TreeNode roots downward
  (generateNewTrees+reconnectRoots+propagateToNextLayer) using a DistanceField
  grid; Layer::convertToLines emits per-surface. ~2000 LOC across
  Generator/Layer/TreeNode/DistanceField + a new make_fills plumbing path —
  a dedicated structural port, not a per-surface emit arm.

## §ccxv FillLightning scope — the one remaining structural port
- Lightning needs Lightning/{Generator,Layer,TreeNode,DistanceField}.cpp
  (~2000 LOC): a per-PrintObject Generator (PrintObject::prepare_lightning_
  infill_data, PrintObject.cpp:1010) that walks all layers top-to-bottom,
  computes m_overhang_per_layer (internal-surface overhang minus walls via
  m_wall_supporting_radius offset), then generateTrees → per-layer
  generateNewTrees + reconnectRoots + TreeNode::propagateToNextLayer (copy,
  realign to below-outlines via EdgeGrid::Grid locator, prune by
  m_prune_length, straighten by m_straightening_max_distance). Layer::
  convertToLines emits per-surface; TreeNode is a shared_ptr/weak_ptr tree
  (Rc<RefCell>/Weak or arena indices in Rust) with addChild/visitBranches/
  getWeightedDistance/localPrune/straighten/hasRecentOverlap/inside.
- Pipeline: ares fill_entities::generate_layer runs per (object,layer) in a
  parallel map; Lightning needs a once-per-object Generator cache (build on
  first lightning layer, then getTreesForLayer(layer_id) per layer) — a new
  data flow, not a per-surface emit arm.
- tpmsfk: ported + compiles; the rsize=0.004mm raster + dense connect_infill
  makes the slice slow (needs ex_tbb-parallel structure / connect perf pass —
  a perf concern, not correctness).

## §ccxvi FillLightning dependency tree mapped — the remaining ~2500 LOC port
- Lightning's transitive deps beyond the 4 Lightning files: DistanceField needs
  sample_grid_pattern (FillRectilinear.cpp:3639) → slice_region_by_vertical_lines
  (SegmentedIntersectionLine rasterizer, FillRectilinear.cpp) + ExPolygonWithOffset
  + Line::distance_to_squared + PointHash — ares has none of the vertical-line
  rasterizer; that helper must be ported too. TreeNode (shared_ptr tree →
  Rc<RefCell>/Weak or arena) + Layer (generateNewTrees/reconnectRoots via
  EdgeGrid::Grid outline locator + SparseNodeGrid PointHash multimap) + Generator
  (per-PrintObject, all-layers top-down overhang + tree propagation).
- marchsq verified FAST standalone (632µs, 1 ring on 20mm): tpmsfk slowness is
  downstream connect_infill/intersection on dense FK rings, not the raster —
  a perf concern, not correctness. tpmsfk is ported + compiles.

Generator must be built on expanded surfaces inside bridge_over_infill and carried to fill_entities (Rc/non-Send) — deep plumbing.

REMAINING for lightning emit:

2. Port two-phase expansion (needs_expansion gate + expand stInternal→stInternalSolid near candidates) targeting the surfaces the Generator reads.

## §ccxxi FillLightning root-on-boundary hypothesis

Propagation fix confirmed; residual: propagated trees keep children=[] on lower layers (realign lifts the single child). Mechanism (identical upstream TreeNode.cpp:202): `lineSegmentPolygonsIntersection(child_p, root_p, ...)` — root ON the internal-surface boundary → segment ends at boundary → intersection d≈0 < resolution*2 → child lifted → single-node tree → convert emits 1pt line → dropped by remove_junction_overlap → lines=0. Upstream identical algorithm → either upstream root is NOT on the boundary edge (grounding point `ground.p()` is supporting_radius-offset inward, or reconnectRoots new_root at ground.p() interior), OR upstream trees are branched so SOME children survive (a branched tree keeps children whose segments don't touch boundary). Next: verify whether upstream new roots sit on-boundary vs interior; check getBestGroundingLocation boundary_location offset; compare tree branching count (ares 7 roots×1child vs upstream expected branching).
